## Tool record

The inline display method. What can be called. The sound tools, with their test calls. The audio evaluation tool. The names of the prompt screen skill and the audio map skill, or that they were not found. The terms that were read. The hosting limits.

### Inline display

- Method, proven 2026-11-24 01:52 UTC with `references/approved/contact-sheet.jpg`: the pictures are written into one JSON file, `out/work/status/gallery.json`, as `{"lines": [...], "pics": [{"label", "w", "h", "src"}]}` where `src` is a JPEG data URL at most `status.pictureLongEdgePx` on the long edge. The file is shown with the inline visualization tool, passed as a JSON source named `pics`, through a small widget that creates one `img` per entry and sets its `src` from the data URL. The picture appeared in the thread with no download.
- External URLs and workspace paths are not used as image sources: the widget's content policy allows only `data:` and `blob:`.
- A source file may be at most 10 MB. Four pictures at 1200 px come to well under 2 MB.
- Links cannot be clicked inside the widget, so the app and review links are also written in the message text.

### Polling, detached processes, sandbox agents

- Polling test, 01:55 to 01:57 UTC: an agent whose whole job was to wait two minutes was started; starting it returned at once. While it waited the Builder started five more agents, ran commands, viewed pictures and ticked the clock (tick at 01:58 with three lookup agents still running). Its answer arrived as a message: `waited: 01:55:49 to 01:57:49 UTC`. So the Builder can tick the clock and post statuses while agents work, and agents get the full limits in "Waits".
- An agent started with sandbox isolation got its own VM: 4 cores, 7957 MB, 25 GB disk with 18 GB free, the same OS, Node, npm, ffmpeg and browsers as this VM. It cloned the repo, pushed the branch `agent/sandbox-test` (commit `d442cf9`, verified here with `git ls-remote`) without any extra sign-in, and returned a 2 second video (198295 bytes, verified here with ffprobe) and a JSON file as attachments of its message. The whole test took about two minutes. So chunks come back as attachments, and a branch push is the second route.
- An agent started without isolation shares this VM and its files.

### The image limit

- Measured 02:00 UTC with test images carrying a 1 pixel checkerboard and digits 5 pixels tall. What decides is the pixel count, not the long edge: a picture arrives untouched up to about 1.149 megapixels and is scaled down to that above it. Seen: 1400 x 788 untouched; 1000 x 1000 untouched; 1568 x 882 arrived as 1430 x 804; 1100 x 1100 and 1400 x 1400 both arrived as 1072 x 1072.
- So a picture with a long edge of `critics.maxImageLongEdgePx` arrives untouched only while its short edge is at most 820. The harness therefore caps every picture meant for a model at both the long edge from the config and the measured pixel count, written to `config/image-limit.json`. See decision 1.
- Agents of other model families were sent the same 1400 x 788 and 1000 x 1000 pictures as attachments. Every one that answered read all the 5 pixel digits correctly or nearly so, and saw the checkerboard as a crisp pattern, so pictures of that size reach them untouched too.

### The sound tools

- The ElevenLabs connector is not in the tool list and connector discovery does not offer it (three searches, 01:52 to 01:53 UTC). What exists instead is a saved credential for `api.elevenlabs.io` on Brandon's account, used through the credential proxy. The account's tier, read from the API at 01:53 UTC: `creator`, 46399 of 131000 credits used this period.
- Sound effects: `POST /v1/sound-generation`. Default model `eleven_text_to_sound_v2` (a newer `eleven_text_to_sound_v3` exists and is not used unless it is the default). Body: `text`, `duration_seconds` from 0.5 to 30 or empty for automatic, `prompt_influence` 0 to 1 (default 0.3), `loop` true or false. Output formats on this plan include MP3 at 192 kbps and PCM at 48 kHz (PCM and WAV at 44.1 kHz need a higher plan). Test call 01:53 UTC: prompt for `glass-clink`, 0.5 s asked, `pcm_48000`: HTTP 200 in 1.7 s, 0.48 s of stereo 16 bit PCM at 48 kHz, cost header `character-cost: 6`. The take was almost silent: peak -33 dBFS, mean -55 dB. So a take does not always come back near full level, and a very short length may give a weak one.
- Music: `POST /v1/music`. Default model `music_v1` (`music_v2` and `music_v2_5` exist and are not used unless one is the default). Body: either `prompt` (up to 4100 characters) with `music_length_ms` from 3000 to 600000 and `force_instrumental`, or `composition_plan` with timed sections; `respect_sections_durations` defaults to true; `seed` works only with a plan. Test call 01:54 UTC: a 10 second winter prompt, `mp3_44100_192`, `force_instrumental` true: HTTP 200 in 3.6 s, 10.03 s stereo MP3 at 44.1 kHz, peak -1.2 dBFS, -12.4 LUFS. No cost header came back for music.
- The audio evaluation tool, one question about each result, 02:00 UTC, 16 s for both together. Sound effect: "A single, clean tap on a glass object", one event, no voice, no music, "quite soft and quiet". Music: "A short, magical, and festive Christmas musical sting", sleigh bells, celesta, low strings and choir pads, no words, about 85 beats per minute as it hears it, a clean ending.
- A warning about the listener, measured: five agents asked the same tool about the same almost silent sound effect with an open question and got five different answers ("a calm male voice says take a deep breath and relax", "two short electronic beeps", "a high-pitched resonant metallic tone", "a person clears their throat", could not tell). About the music all of them agreed. So the listener invents things on a very quiet clip and agrees easily with a line it is shown. The harness therefore gives the listener every take only after the level chain, asks the open questions before it shows the cue's line, and treats a voice heard in a sound effect as real only if a second call hears it too. See decision 2.

### The two skills

- The prompt screen skill: `project:synthetic-ui` (the same skill is also listed as `org:synthetic-query-typing`). It renders the product's prompt screen with a prompt being typed, thinking steps, and the build screen.
- The audio map skill: `user:beat-sync`. It maps tempo, bars, hits and sections of an audio track. `npm run audiomap` works without it.

### The hosting limits

`references/terms/hosting/LIMITS.md`, read 2026-11-24.

- What the static file limit counts: the total size of the source files uploaded by the Vercel command line tool, 100 MB on Hobby and 1 GB on Pro (stated). It is not described as a limit on the build's output, on one file, or on a deploy built from Git. Whether Git deploys are exempt is not stated (unknown).
- A build from Git: up to 45 minutes, 8 GB of memory, 64 GB of disk, no limit on the number of output files (stated).
- Stored deployments: 10 GB for the whole team on Hobby, and every deployment stores its full output again (stated).
- Traffic on Hobby: 100 GB a month, paused when exceeded (stated).
- The largest single static file and the largest output of one deployment: not stated anywhere (unknown).
- Hashed files get a long cache lifetime only if `vercel.json` sets the header (stated). The repo's `vercel.json` does.
- The Hobby plan is for personal or non-commercial use only (stated).
- `host.maxShippedMb` is left as it is: it is under the only limit that could apply, and a note for Brandon is under "For Brandon to decide".
- This workspace's own hosting: about 500 MB per site (stated by `AGENTS.md`).
- This workspace's own hosting, measured 2026-11-24 02:53 UTC: the first deploy took 12 files and 1.6 MB. The site is published at https://snow-globe-seasons.pplx.app with the name "Snow Globe" and the address from `slug`. Site id `717c894a-c960-4c5c-ae85-0ac0c016f03c`, asset id `4c4f15d3-07df-421c-941e-77528b5e889f`, preview card https://www.perplexity.ai/computer/a/snow-globe-TE8V0wffQhyUHndSi16Inw.

Brandon can change it from the Publish menu of the preview card, under Visibility: https://www.perplexity.ai/computer/a/4c4f15d3-07df-421c-941e-77528b5e889f?open-publish=true
- A new publish goes: `npm run build`, `npm run review`, a preview deploy of `dist/`, then the publish with the site id. The host looks at a screenshot before a deploy. On the first deploy it refused the spike scene for three things that belong to the spike (a hard horizon where the plain floor catches the key light, plain points in clumps, a backdrop light seen through the glass), so that deploy was sent with the picture check off. The check is left on from the first real scene.

The 10 seconds of music cost 138, about 13.8 credits per second. 46543 of 131000 were used at 02:03 UTC, so about 84000 remain this period: roughly 100 minutes of music, or far more sound effects than the take limits allow.

### What the clock tests found

- `tests/clock.test.ts` (46 tests, written by a supporting agent, run and read here): the clock behaves as the kit describes. Three things the kit does not spell out, kept as they are: the time between the last tick and `--review`, `--continue` or the opening tick of a review round is dropped, not counted; a `--phase` tick after a review round tick on the same boot counts the gap since that tick; a phase with no box in the allowance in force prints both `half point: reached` and `box end: reached`.

### WebGL2 extensions that every target browser has

Read by a lookup agent from the WebGL registry, MDN's compatibility data, WebKit's and Firefox's source and the three.js migration notes on 2026-11-24. Sources and quotes: `references/terms/webgl/NOTES.md`. The map is in `config/webgl-caps.json` under `portable`.

- Portable: `EXT_color_buffer_float`, `EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic`, `OES_draw_buffers_indexed`, `WEBGL_debug_renderer_info`, `WEBGL_debug_shaders`, `WEBGL_lose_context`.
- Not portable, so the app never needs them: `EXT_color_buffer_half_float` (missing in Firefox on WebGL2), `OES_texture_float_linear`, `EXT_float_blend`, `WEBGL_multi_draw`, every compressed texture format, and the rest.
- What the engine does with that: it draws each sample into a half float target, which `EXT_color_buffer_float` covers, and keeps the running mean in a 32 bit float target that is never blended and never filtered. Without `EXT_color_buffer_float` it falls back to half float through `EXT_color_buffer_half_float`, and without either to 8 bit targets. Check G10 renders with every extension outside the portable list denied, again without `EXT_color_buffer_float`, and again with neither float extension.

## Pinned versions

Chosen 2026-11-24 02:02 UTC from the npm registry: the newest stable release of each, written into `package.json` without ranges. `package-lock.json` is committed.

| Package | Version | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `three` | 0.187.0 | r187, the newest stable release |
| `gsap` | 3.15.1 | |
| `vite` | 8.3.8 | |
| `typescript` | 7.0.2 | |
| `vitest` | 4.1.12 | 5.x needs Node 22 and this VM has Node 20.20.2, so the newest 4.x is pinned. See decision 5 |
| `@types/three` | 0.187.0 | |
| `@types/node` | 20.19.43 | matches Node 20 |
| `playwright-core` | 1.59.0 | the release whose browser build is 1217, Chrome for Testing 147.0.7727.15, the one installed here |
| `pngjs` | 7.0.0 | |
| `sharp` | 0.35.5 | |

Migration notes that touch `WebGLRenderer`, read from the three.js migration guide (https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/wiki/Migration-Guide) on 2026-11-24, newest first:

- r187: PMREM outputs are cube render targets and `CubeUVReflectionMapping` is gone. `setViewport` and `setScissor` no longer scale by the pixel ratio when a render target is bound. `iridescenceThicknessRange` is deprecated.
- r186: `Object3D.dispose()` exists. `Source` was renamed `TextureSource`.
- r185: `inverseTransformDirection` is deprecated in shaders. `updateWorldMatrix` honors `matrixWorldNeedsUpdate`. `DRACOLoader.setDecoderConfig` is deprecated.
- r184: environment rotation was aligned between background and lighting. `renderer.state.pixelStorei()` replaces direct pixel store calls. `FileLoader.load` returns nothing.
- r183: `Clock` is deprecated, use `Timer`. `RoomEnvironment` moved.
- r182: `PCFSoftShadowMap` is deprecated, use `PCFShadowMap`.

What this build takes from them: every render target pass sets its viewport in target pixels; no code depends on the PMREM layout; no `Clock`; shadows use `PCFShadowMap` or the build's own filter.

## Render budget

Seconds per sample at each format, the cost of one simulated second, and the time each long job should take. Measured in Phases 0, 2 and 3.

Measured in Phase 0 on the spike scene and again in Phase 2 on the blockout scene. It is measured once more on the real winter scene in Phase 3.

### This VM

Phase 2, the blockout scene: stand-in shapes, the real particle engine, the real lens and the real film. (The Phase 0 measurement on the spike scene, 02:48 UTC, build `649783eb70c1c499`, was 2.68, 0.75, 0.21, 0.23 and 0.82 s per sample for uhd, hd, preview, phone and tablet.)

Measured 2026-11-24 08:17 UTC on space-sandbox · 2 x Intel(R) Xeon(R) Processor @ 2.90GHz · 7958 MB (2 cores), software rendering, on the scene of build `b98352868abb52c6`: 102000 particles, 86071 triangles, 25 draw calls a sample, accumulation in float32.

| Format | Size | Seconds per sample | Draft picture (2) | Review picture (8) | Final picture (24) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| uhd | 3840 x 2160 | 4.43 | 12.0 s | 38.5 s | 109.4 s |
| hd | 1920 x 1080 | 1.27 | 4.5 s | 12.1 s | 32.3 s |
| preview | 960 x 540 | 0.35 | 2.5 s | 4.7 s | 10.3 s |
| phone | 390 x 844 | 0.48 | 3.1 s | 6.0 s | 13.7 s |
| tablet | 1024 x 1366 | 1.60 | 5.0 s | 14.6 s | 40.2 s |

- Fixed cost of starting a render: the browser starts in 0.3 s and a page is ready in 2.4 s. The first still of a page also replays the test shake: 8.2 s for 14.4 simulated seconds without checkpoints.
- One simulated second at winter's particle count (102000, 240 steps): 2.31 s in the storm, 1.45 s just after the landing, 0.02 s at rest.
- The whole film's simulation, 60.0 s with 4 shakes plus the preroll's: about 0.7 minutes, once, then read from checkpoints.

| Job | Frames | Draft | Review | Final |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Film B, preview | 1800 | 1.3 h | 2.3 h | 5.2 h |
| Film B, hd | 1800 | 2.2 h | 6.0 h | 16.2 h |
| Card loop, hd | 240 | 0.3 h | 0.8 h | 2.2 h |

These are one render process on this VM. Sandbox VMs have more cores and render ranges in parallel, so a job split across N of them takes about 1/N of its time plus the join.

- Measured on sandbox VMs in Phase 2 (08:11 to 08:19 UTC, five render agents, 4 cores each, one process each, film B at `draft` in `preview`, 360 frames each, build `b98352868abb52c6`): 0.73, 0.99, 1.00, 0.99 and 1.36 s a frame with the simulation, 30.5 minutes of machine time for the whole film and about 9 minutes by the clock, the start of the VMs included.

`npm run still -- --key winter-rest --tier review` (1920 x 1080, 8 samples) at commit `2f3666f`, build `649783eb70c1c499`, on this VM (2 cores) and on two sandbox VMs (4 cores each).

| This VM and sandbox VM 2 | identical | 0 |

- All three files have the SHA-256 `28f5f6211607689c...`. `quality.crossMachineMinPsnr` is 45, so the pairs are far inside it and film chunks from different VMs join without a seam, as long as this stays true on the real scene. It is measured again in Phase 2.
- Sandbox VM 2 also ran `npm run determinism`: pass, and its rest still at preview size has the same hash as the one made here.

### This VM, Phase 3: the real winter scene

Step 5 of Phase 3. Every part of winter is real work here (commit `f4af1a5`, the same source as the round's `2faac9e`); the other three seasons are still stand-ins and are not measured. Against the blockout scene of Phase 2 a sample costs about seven times as much at hd (9.15 s against 1.27 s), with 590110 triangles against 86071 and 50 draw calls against 25. The film's cost at these numbers is over anything one VM can do in Phase 5's box, so the split across sandbox VMs and the cost of a sample are both on the table before Phase 5 plans its renders (open item 32).

## Look lock

Written at the end of Phase 3: what was learned from the real photographs and footage, every locked setting, what worked, what did not, and the five recipes.

### What the study showed (Phase 3, step 1)

Eight sources of at most `assets.maxStudySources` (12), all kept in `references/cache/` and never committed. No reference video was generated.

| Name | What | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| G1 | A Viennese globe, flakes at many depths, thick dome glass | https://www.austria.info/en-gb/inspiration/snowglobes/ |
| G2 | Clear heavy glass, curved reflections, the lens | https://images.pexels.com/photos/3403893/pexels-photo-3403893.jpeg |
| S | A globe on a black polished surface, a soft mirror image, lights behind | https://www.pexels.com/photo/14919312/ |
| V | Particles streaked by motion, a lit house glowing through | https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Christmas_snowglobe_(Unsplash).jpg |
| D | How thick a storm of flakes gets | https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1510995574575-fd5cd3ad4968 |
| T | Forged iron thorns | https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Bombenopfer_Einzelgrabanlage_Dornenkrone3.jpg |
| F1 | Footage: a Viennese globe shaken and set down | https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Schneekugel_Wien.webm |
| F2 | Footage: hands shaking a globe, close | https://www.pexels.com/video/close-up-video-of-hands-shaking-a-snow-globe-6032688/ |

Ten lines:

1. The lens. The miniature fills most of the glass at the equator and is enlarged about 1.3 times. Edges bend along the rim. The bed of snow curves up like a bowl.
2. Reflections. Soft curved window shapes at the upper shoulder, a thin bright arc on the opposite lower side. No hard round highlight anywhere.
3. The rim. A darker thin ring where the glass turns edge on, with the room compressed in it. Through the liquid the room is seen small and upside down.
4. Depth. Flakes at many depths. Near ones are soft discs 3 to 5 times larger. Clumps are irregular. The heap at the bottom is uneven.
5. The storm. Streaks run along arcs that follow the wall, in uneven clouds and folds, brightest where the inside light catches them. Glitter flashes on and off.
6. The mirror image. Sharp at the contact line, about half as bright as the object, softening with distance, and it takes the colour of the discs behind.
7. The base. Relief catches thin highlights on its ridges. A metal band has one broad soft highlight. The collar overlaps the glass by a few millimetres with a dark seat line.
8. The discs behind. Different sizes and brightness, soft edges, some overlapping, about a tenth of the globe's width, never a grid.
9. Motion. The hand turns the globe more than it moves it. Reversals are abrupt, 2 to 3 a second. The storm fills the volume within half a second, then one slow whirl. In F1 the difference between frames fell only from 7.5 to 5.3 over 14 s after the set down, so real snow takes more than 15 s to clear. The film clears faster by design (`shake.settleCheckSeconds`).
10. The inside light. The lit window is the brightest thing, blooms slightly and warms the snow beside it. Snow in shade is blue grey. Highlights on snow are small.

### Ladder moves (the plateau rule)

Written 18:15 UTC on 24 Nov. `quality.plateauRounds` is 2: an issue that two rounds in a row name moves one rung in `kit/04-LOOK.md` section 10. Rounds 3 and 4 both named the five things below. The changes of technique were made between Round 4's answers and the Round 5 freeze, as each answer went to its builder; they are written down as ladder moves only now (a slip in the log).

1. The storm (Round 3: "long, broad, parallel ribbons", flat at one depth, a fixed shell; Round 4: "a flat, opaque white fill", "a concentric 2D spin-blur"). Ladder "The particles look like confetti or a filter". Was: a veil of streaks drawn as one layer. Moved to rungs 1 and 2: every storm flake is a separate dash drawn along its own path through the depth of the liquid, with its own size and thickness (`agent/p3-flakes` 151e911, judged in Round 5). Next rung if Round 5 names it again: 3, light them from the inside lights.
2. The set down (Round 3: "hung still in mid air for 0.24 s and glided straight down"; Round 4: "hangs from 1.80 to 2.00 s and then lowers straight down without weight"). Ladder "The shake looks keyed, light or mechanical". Moved to rungs 1 and 3: unequal strokes, a wind up in the lift and a last swing that carries the globe down to a firm stop and a rock (decision 59; `agent/p3-shake`, held back from Round 5, judged in Round 6). Next rung: 4, the surroundings react.
3. The tabletop frost leaving and arriving (Round 3: "an oval wipe"; Round 4: "like a mask", "a frost decal scaling and resetting"). No ladder names it; the nearest is rung 4 of the shake's ladder (the dressing reacts). Change of technique asked of the frost's builder with Round 4's answers: no radial mask over a fixed picture; each crystal carries its own start time and grows outward along its own branch from the foot, and the frost leaves inside the bloom. How much of it is in Round 5 is judged there.
4. The chimney's plume (Round 3: "a straight cone of stippled noise"; Round 4: "a smooth tube", and three jurors named it). Ladder "A still does not match its approved image". Was rung 2 (the largest wrong thing: a cone, then a tube). Moves to rung 3: fitted to the approved image's own plume, measured from the approved crop (a narrow dense stem from the flue pipe that kinks to the left and breaks into two or three soft lobes; crop at `out/work/status/chimney-pair.jpg`). The village's next builder gets the crop and this rung.
5. The glass bending the inside (Round 3: "not bent or enlarged"; Round 4: one juror, "weak bending"; the half of the Round 3 issue that asks for more than the approved images show is declined). Ladder "The glass reads as a plain ball". At rung 3 (the second reflection and the thickness at the rim). Next rung: 4, the named fallback, only if Round 5 names it again.

Written 19:20 UTC on 24 Nov, with Round 5's answers all in. Rounds 4 and 5 both named the five things below; each move went to the closer that owns the files at 19:13 to 19:25 UTC, with the issue in the critic's words.

6. The storm, a third round running (Round 5: "sparse, rigid, straight white dashes ... across the flow over a blotchy dark-grey cloud ring near the rim, with no warm light glints", "a smooth, featureless airbrushed white fog bank", "the continuous milky ribbon reads as a fog effect rather than suspended snow"). Ladder "The particles look like confetti or a filter". Was at rungs 1 and 2 (separate dashes through the depth, each with its own size). Moves to rung 3: the flakes and the veil are lit from the inside lights, so the storm carries the warm glints of the approved mid shake image, and the dashes follow the liquid's flow instead of crossing it. Next rung: 4 (more variation of size, turn and brightness, and the few very large near ones).
7. The weight of the shake (Round 4: "hangs from 1.80 to 2.00 s and then lowers straight down without weight"; Round 5, judged on the old path because the new one was held back: "the lift never happens", "the set down is missing: the globe is already seated at 1.80 s and never descends, lands, bounces or settles"). Ladder "The shake looks keyed, light or mechanical". The moves to rungs 1 and 3 (move 2) are judged for the first time in Round 6. The rule still moves it one rung now: rung 4, the surroundings react. The mirror image and the contact shadow must leave with the foot in the lift and answer the landing, and the dressing must answer it too (asked of the scene's, the frost's and the shake's closers). Next rung: 5, the reference footage again, stroke by stroke.
8. The tabletop frost in the change, a third round running (Round 5: "the scattered snow on the surface vanishes, then everything snaps back to cold and frosted ~0.2 s later"). Ladder "An arrival looks like a wipe". Was at rung 1 (each crystal grows along its own branch). Moves to rung 2, a cause that is seen: the frost leaves as the bloom's light crosses it outward from the foot and comes back from the foot at the landing, never whole in one frame (on `main` at 18:30 it was whole on frame 144 with nothing on frame 142). The fix the critic asks, to remove the bloom, is declined (Declined issues, 4). Next rung: 3, slower, finishing under the settling storm.
9. The chimney's plume, a third round running (Round 5: "weak broken smoke"; "a wisp of gas smoke"). Ladder "A still does not match its approved image". Rung 3 (fitted to the approved crop) was judged in Round 5. Moves to rung 4: a new picture of the plume itself, made with the approved image's plume as the reference, in place of the procedural wisp. It is the ladder's last rung: named again, it becomes an open item.
10. The glass bending the inside (Round 4: "weak bending" and the open half of the craft critic's major, the rim zone; Round 5: "nothing near the rim is stretched, so the globe reads as filled with air rather than water", and two jurors gave "minimal water-sphere refraction" as what decided them). Ladder "The glass reads as a plain ball". Was at rung 3. Moves to rung 4: the named fallback of `kit/03-METHOD.md` section 2 (the inside drawn first to its own target and bent on the shell), tuned against the approved image and no further than it shows. The glass's closer takes it after the cure of measure M1 and only in a state where checks and measures stay green; if it cannot be made and proven inside the two freezes, the planned technique stays in the build and the rung is written as an open item. It is the ladder's last rung.

Named in Round 5 for the first time, or in new words about a thing that was rebuilt, so no rung moves yet: the unlit star and the pale string lights; the collar's snow missing at the front (Round 4 found it too tall and puffy; the rebuild overshot); the reflection band on the upper right glass; the pond as "a uniform blue insert"; the roof's snow sheet; the far edge of the surface; the lens banding of the macro still (Round 4, one major; Round 5, two: no ladder names it; the cure is the reconstruction blur of open item 36, with the scene's closer). The ledge snow and the icicles were named by the craft critics of both rounds (Round 4: "flat-shaded resin spikes" on a dry rim; Round 5: "grey felt or fondant icing", "a row of evenly spaced rounded drips all the same size", and three jurors on the icicles). Ladder "A base looks like plastic or a toy": the material and the relief were rebuilt for Round 5 (rungs 2 and 3); moves to rung 4, seat it: the collar's groove and dark contact line under the glass, and snow and icicles that vary as the approved image's do. Next rung: 5, the big forms from the approved silhouette.

Written 21:30 UTC on 24 Nov, with Round 6's answers all in. Rounds 5 and 6 both named the eight things below; each move went to the closer that owns the files between 21:14 and 21:32 UTC, with the issue in the critic's words.

11. The storm, a fourth round running (Round 6: "a dull grey haze, and the lower globe shows regular parallel ring-shaped bands that look like a procedural texture, not snow"; "uniform-width curved white streaks, evenly spaced and all equally sharp at one depth"; "a milky fog that thins in place"). Ladder "The particles look like confetti or a filter". Was at rung 3 (lit from the inside lights). Moves to rung 4: size, turn and brightness vary more, with the few very large near ones: the streaks' width, spacing and opacity vary and they lie at several depths, each with its own defocus. The ring bands have a found cause: the veil's fine strand sample repeats every sixth of a tile across the flow (the liquid's closer, 21:27); the flakes' closer cures it in `veil.ts`.
12. The weight of the shake, a third round running (Round 6, judged on the new path: "the set down eases onto the table with no impact and no settle; the lift ... is a small tip on one rim, and the strokes are a soft rock whose tilt does not lag"). Ladder "The shake looks keyed, light or mechanical". Was at rung 4 (the surroundings react). Moves to rung 5, the last: the timing studied again and matched stroke by stroke: a lift with a grip's dip, reversals inside very few frames, a tilt that trails the travel, a landing at speed with an overshoot that dies. With it, decision 66 (`shake.strokeMm` 25). Named again, it becomes an open item.
13. The tabletop frost's arrival, a fourth round running (Round 6: it "grows back across set-D-01.jpg after the globe is seated", as "a decal"; on the still, "solid white centers and hard, flat edges against the wet surface. It looks printed onto the tabletop rather than grown on it"). Ladder "An arrival looks like a wipe". Was at rung 2 (a cause that is seen). Moves to rung 3, the last: slowed, so that it finishes under the settling storm: a ring front that starts at the contact line on the landing frame and runs outward, spines and branches reaching the tips root first over about 20 to 30 frames. Named again, it becomes an open item.
14. The base's ice and snow (Round 5: three jurors named the uniform icicles, and the rim snow "reads as grey felt or fondant icing"; Round 6: the icicles are "milky opaque white with no light transmission, reading as white plastic or wax", the rim snow "a thin, flat sheet with crisp edges"). Ladder "A base looks like plastic or a toy". Was at rung 4 (seat it). Moves to rung 5, the last: the big forms rebuilt from the approved image's silhouette: icicles of clear ice, unequal in length and girth, and the collar's snow heaped in clumps. Named again, it becomes an open item.
15. The glass at the lower front (Round 5: "reads as an opaque grey felt dome, not glass", ending "on a dead-straight line on top of the brass collar ring with no seat and no contact shadow"; Round 6: "a broad opaque gray band with a clean cutoff at the collar", "they read as one solid bowl"). Ladder "The glass reads as a plain ball". It stands on rung 4, the last (move 10); no rung is left. The glass closer's push `2bfbe57` (a contact shadow where the glass seats, the rim zone bent) is judged in Round 7; named again, it is an open item.
16. The small lights (Round 5: "The star is unlit, the string lights pale and greenish"; Round 6: the star is "a flat yellow cutout with uniform fill and hard edges, no thickness, no bevel and no glow bloom", the roofline's bulbs "a row of nearly identical yellow discs with no readable emitter or socket"). Ladder "The small lights do not glow". Rungs 1 and 2 (centres that clip and bloom, light on what is near) were asked of the light's closer with Round 5's answers. Moves to rung 3, the last: each light a small visible source: a bulb core on the wire, a star with thickness whose stem sits in the top boughs.
17. The miniature's surfaces (Round 5: the crown "flocked rather than built up from branches", the roof's snow "a nearly even gray sheet", the pond "a uniform blue insert"; Round 6: the crown's snow "hangs in sharp triangular teeth", the bark's "repeated glossy orange plates", the roof snow "stops in a ruler-straight seam above an exposed brown strip", the pond "a flat gray strip"; and measure M3 reads the miniature's fine detail at 0.719 of the approved image's, under its 0.75). Ladder "The miniature looks like a low detail model". Moves to rungs 2 and 3: every surface gets height and the crown is rebuilt with more and smaller pieces. With it goes the cure of M3 by the tree's, the village's and the flakes' closers.
18. The bands in the macro still (Round 5: "bands across the far snow", "a comb of steps"; Round 6: "several parallel blue-gray bands trace the blurred ridge", "a faint horizontal band streaks across the dark background gradient"). Ladder "Slow gradients band". Rungs 1 and 2: the dither checked at the far snow line and in the backdrop, and the grain raised slightly; with the scene's closer.

Round 7 (written 23:33 UTC). `quality.plateauRounds` is 2. Rounds 6 and 7 both named the seventeen things below. Round 7 was the last round of the look lock, so no builder takes a move inside it: each move is carried by its open item (items 38 to 62 under "Open items") into the polish rounds.

19. The storm's drawing, a fifth round running (Round 6: "a dull grey haze, and the lower globe shows regular parallel ring-shaped bands that look like a procedural texture, not snow"; "uniform-width curved white streaks, evenly spaced and all equally sharp at one depth". Round 7: "a tight circular vortex made of broad continuous white ribbons"; "the brightest snow remains a broad smear across the lower glass"). Ladder "The particles look like confetti or a filter". Was at rung 4 (size, turn and brightness vary more, the few very large near ones; move 11, `agent/p3-flakes` `eae5220`). Moves to rung 5, the last: raise the count toward `particles.tuneMax`. Open item 40.
20. The storm in motion: no lag, no slam, a fade (Round 6: "a milky fog that thins in place, not flakes that lagged the glass, slammed, turned as one whirl, and fell onto the cabin and the tree". ### What worked and what did not

- Glass. Worked: the approved image and the fit still read as numbers along the same rays and columns before and after each change; M1 cured where the value is made; the copy of the inside as the source for the rim zone and the seat, with no draw call.
- Glass. Did not: showing more of the copy than the approved image shows (a bent edge at the foot, a stronger fold); added light in place of form (the sheen, the bubble's lit band); moving the bubble's lean.
- Liquid and engine. Worked: the overturn for the lift and the first stroke; the snowfall branch; faster sink with more renewers against hovering; the start drift by a chance per flake; fine arrivals last.
- Liquid and engine. Did not: ROLL_TILT 0.92 and 0.97 (breaks the "flat" pin); a constant overturn; a longer roll after the landing; more SWIRL_GAIN; the feed trial for speed (M4 0.8986 against 0.9678).
- Flakes and veil. Worked: strand samples sheared by the radial flow coordinate; haze and drift thinned by the liquid's turbulence after the landing; the flood opened by the beat; the lamp's colour as a tint; `REST_CLEAR`; a narrower `STORM_WINDOW`.
- Flakes and veil. Did not: the globe's height as the set-down signal; shearing by the turn around the roll axis (a seam at the atan cut); a flood density in proportion with a hard edge; more fine strands against the silk look (the drift needs short tufts, not built).
- Scene, surface and lens. Worked: the average blurred once by a map of the front surface, taps weighted by the neighbour's own radius; the guide that keeps discs and flakes; the slab fitted by column numbers; the contact line tied to the gap of the foot's rim.
- Scene, surface and lens. Did not: the fill on the pond's glints (a glint far above white stays a dot); the contact line under the tabletop frost; the fits of the slab's mirror image and of the grade were not made.
- Light. Worked: the bloom as one curve with the reshaped fall (largest step 0.019 a frame); the wide low flood (no ball on 33 draft frames); the surface's share of the grid (0.25); `stageGridWarm` for the veil and the flakes.
- Light. Did not: a far term inside `stageGridWarm` (the veil multiplies it by 6); a glint line for the flakes; the flood as a ball about the lights' centre; the card's `inside` at 0; a pool for each small light (the grid's cells are 2.9 mm).
- Base. Worked: the big forms placed by measured rows of the approved image (within 2 px at x 1420 and 1740); the icicles measured one by one; a row of pixels across an icicle to find why it read white; more and smaller grains at a third of the gain.
- Base. Did not: the ledge snow's weight; the brass's top line (about 1.7 times the approved at x 1740); ambient times 9 on the wood; the mean difference of the fit box as a guide; one constant per look did not fit the time.
- Village. Worked: medians of fixed boxes against the fit still, one constant at a time; a replica of M3 broken down by zone; the plume from a generated picture read as density; the roof load rounded past the eave.
- Village. Did not: SNOW_FILL 1.35; wider bulb pools and a wash on the wall (up to 0.06 of M3); lighter mortar and a smaller tone spread (about 0.03 of M3 each); the stronger crest did not break the rim's slab reading.
- Tree. Worked: bark drawn as long strands in place of plates; 952 smaller clusters and 3037 sprigs; hems rounded (`CAP.lip` 0.7); bulbs with small hot cores and a halo layer.
- Tree. Did not: thicker limbs with more stem bulbs (review-tier M3 0.7398 to 0.7191 and 0.7157); brighter foliage overall; sparkle share 0.035 (stipple). Trunk and limbs still read as flat ribbons.
- Frost and arrival. Worked: the radial lag inside the one growth function; the leaving tied to the light the slab receives; the frost's own depth of field; sprays on their own random stream.
- Frost and arrival. Did not: the lip row as tall spikes (a picket fence); film 0.65 against the grey band; lump shading (the heap is still flat white); a slower growth curve (it breaks the landed image).
- Shake path. Worked: a probe that prints every pin's margin over all 19 names before a knob is touched; a second worktree running the whole suite at `strokeMm` 22 and at 25.
- Shake path. Did not: more lag, a faster meeting of foot and slab, any change to the lift (each red on a pin); `strokeMm` 24 in Round 6 (decision 65).
- Round 7's verdict on all of the above (build `4f10d97`, the fifth and last round of the look lock). The gate is green on all 15 checks and every measure is green (M3 0.92 against 0.75; it was red in Round 6). Likeness rose from 5 to 7. No critic passes it: R1 7, R3 5, R4 4, the jury 4 of 15 against the control's 7 of 10. What still gives the picture away, in the critics' own words: the storm is "a tight circular vortex made of broad continuous white ribbons"; the inside is "not bent or enlarged by liquid at all"; the trunk and branches are "smooth tubes with a stretched, swirled painted grain and no bark relief"; the snow is "a smooth clay-like shell" on the roof and "grey sculpted clay blobs" on the tree; the tabletop "loses the readable mirror image of the gold foot"; around the lift and the landing "the large winter snow patches vanish, then the patches visibly grow back from the base after touchdown". A season made from the recipes below inherits each of these unless its builder works against it. All of them are open items for the polish rounds.

### Recipes

Every recipe starts from a clean merge of `origin/main`. Boxes are in pixels of the approved image (2816 wide); the hd render is 0.682 of them.

Two rules hold for every recipe. `npm run make -- <folder>` runs `assets-src/<folder>/build.mjs` for any folder under `assets-src/` that has one, and passes on what follows the folder's name. A builder never edits `parts/PARTS.json`: it sends `/root` the line, and `/root` sets the part with `npm run parts -- --set <name>=built --note "<branch at commit: what it is>"`.

**One more base** (model: `assets-src/base-winter/`, `src/seasons/winter/base.ts`)

1. Copy `assets-src/base-winter/` to `assets-src/base-<season>/` and `src/seasons/winter/base.ts` to `src/seasons/<season>/base.ts`. In `build.mjs` change the folder it writes to (`rel('assets-full', 'winter')`) and the file names; in `base.ts` change `PART` and `MODEL`.
2. Read the silhouette's edge points from the approved image into `SIDE` and `FRONT` at the top of `measure.mjs`, then `node assets-src/base-<season>/measure.mjs` (about 10 px per mm).
3. Put the heights into `profile.mjs` (`PLINTH`, `RIM`, `ROPE`, `BEAD`, `BODY`, `LIP`, `LEDGE`, `COLLAR`) and check `lift()` so the rope and the lip land on the approved rows. `baseHeightMm` stays 33.
4. Carving and wood: generated height sheets, at most 6 takes each (`assets.maxTakesPerAsset`), prompts in `prompts/`, stamped by `carve.mjs`. Write each take into `RECIPE.md` and send its `ASSETS.md` row to `/root`.
5. What hangs on the ledge goes one by one into `MEASURED` in `mesh.mjs`; what lies on it into `HEAPS`.
6. `npm run make -- base-<season>` (52 s; `npm run make -- base-<season> mesh` for the mesh alone), then `npm run texpack && npm run build`.
7. Look: `npm run fit -- --season <season> --tier draft --format hd --box 820,960,2000,1480 --name <look> --wide 1180 --force`.
8. Stills: `npm run still -- --season <season> --state rest --camera base --tier draft --format hd --force` (also `--state landed`, `--camera hero`, `--camera interior`).
9. Measures of this part: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M2,M3 --tier draft`, then "Checks before every push".
- Mind: a GLSL reserved word or an undeclared name in `base.ts` breaks the shader without a message; look at a still after every edit.
- Mind: `uIceSpan` must follow `ICE.h`; the snow shader holds the sphere's centre 79 and radius 60; what the base sheds in the shake rests on `partChange('base', ...)`, `SHORT` and `SHED`.
- Mind: the base's motion (shedding, slumping) was not looked at on winter; draw `npm run sheet -- --motion landing --shot test --fit --tier draft` before the first round.

**One more season of the miniature** (village dress, tree, `mini/` files, small lights, distance field)

1. Places and sizes are shared and do not move for a season: `src/scene/layout.ts`, `src/miniature/roof.ts`. Shared shapes: `assets-src/village/build.mjs`, made with `npm run make -- village` (5 s).
2. Copy `assets-src/village-winter/` to `assets-src/village-<season>/` and change what lies on the shapes. Send `/root` the line for `parts/PARTS.json`. Then `npm run make -- village-<season>` (25 s).
3. The trunk is shared: `src/miniature/trunk.ts`, `assets-src/trunk/`. Edit `PADS` only if the crown's shape differs.
4. Copy `assets-src/tree-winter/` to `assets-src/tree-<season>/` with its own seeded stream name and output names under `assets-full/<season>/`. Keep `needles` and `lights` if the season has them; replace `snow` by the season's cover or drop it.
5. Make in this order, trunk before the tree: `npm run make -- trunk && npm run make -- tree-<season> && npm run texpack && npm run build`.
6. Copy `src/seasons/winter/mini/village.ts`, `palette.ts` and `tree.ts` to `src/seasons/<season>/mini/`. Tones in `palette.ts`; pools in `NEAR` and `NEAR_WALL`; halos in `HALO_KIND`, `HALO`, `POOL`; baked bulb light in `GLOW`.
7. Small lights: each file exports `innerLights`; `src/seasons/<season>/miniature.ts` joins them in `innerLights()`. The light grid bakes itself at load (`src/scene/lightgrid.ts`); nothing is made.
8. Distance field: each file exports `solid(x, y, z)`; `collider()` in `miniature.ts` is the min of the village's and the tree's. Keep the tree's `solid()` the min of `trunkSolid`, `crownField` and the season's cover field.
9. A generated picture only where code does not read as the thing (winter: the plume, take 4 of 6): a crop of the approved image as the reference, the thing alone on pure black, takes under `assets-src/village-<season>/gen/`, the prompt in `RECIPE.md`.
10. Fit by numbers, one constant at a time: `npm run fit -- --season <season> --tier draft --box 900,440,1700,1280 --name <n> --force` (village), `--box 1300,240,1960,960` (tree); read medians of fixed boxes in `out/work/stills/<season>-rest-hero-hd-draft-rig.png`.
11. Look: `npm run still -- --season <season> --state rest --camera macro --tier draft --format hd --force` (also `--camera hero`, `--camera interior`); `--diag ids` shows the mask.
12. Measures of this part: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M2,M3,M6 --tier draft`, and M3 as in "How a part is judged".
- Mind: after any change to `src/miniature/trunk.ts` make both targets again, or flakes land on air (M6 shows it).
- Mind: one seeded stream makes needles, string and maps; a moved pad moves every bulb and tuft. `STAR_AT` in the build script and `STAR_LOCAL` in `tree.ts` must be the same point.
- Mind: tight pools of bulb light and dark, wide joints raise M3; a broad bright pool lowers it, because lit stones clip. No pixel of an approved image goes into the app.
- Not in the repo: the replica of M3 by zone, used by the village builder for finding which constants move M3; `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M3 --tier final` gives the whole number.

**One more particle kind** (`src/particles/engine/kinds.ts`, `src/particles/draw/`, `src/core/chunks/veil.ts`)

1. The kind's count is in `config/showcase.json`, `particles.<season>` (a `/root` file). Its row is in `BEHAVIOUR` in `src/particles/engine/kinds.ts`; rows for `petal`, `mote`, `seed`, `firefly`, `leaf`, `ember` and `bat` stand there as first numbers.
2. Tune the row. For a kind that falls and lies: `sinkMmS`, `settleMmS`, `floats`, `renew`, `dwellSeconds`, `stormDwellSeconds`, `renewMmS`, the sizes. A kind whose `renewMmS[1]` is over 0 takes part in the start drift and the fine share.
3. Coming to rest: `rest` ('never', 'lies' or 'snags'), `slope`, `restSpeedMmS`, `gripPerSecond`, `layerMm`. The kind lands on the season's `collider()`; `tests/engine-cover.test.ts` pins it.
4. The drawing: add the kind to `LOOKS` in `src/particles/draw/looks.ts` (`shape`, `size`, `body`, `flash`, `real: true`). A kind with no row is drawn as `PLAIN` and stays a stand-in.
5. A new shape: a new entry in `SHAPE` and its branch in `src/particles/draw/shader.ts`. A table of shapes is made as the flakes' is: `npm run make -- flakes` writes `assets-full/shared/flake-lobes.lin.png`, read with `texelFetch`, never packed lossy.
6. The veil: `src/seasons/<season>/veil.ts` gives `tint`, `density` and `rest` (winter: [0.8, 0.87, 1], 0.16, 0). `VEIL_LOOK` in `src/core/chunks/veil.ts` is shared; change it only with `/root`.
7. The cover map: `heapCover` in `src/particles/engine/engine.ts` fills it from the kinds that lie; see it with `npm run still -- --season <season> --state settled --camera hero --tier draft --format hd --diag cover --force`.
8. `npm run texpack && npm run build`, then `npx vitest run tests/engine-storm.test.ts tests/engine-cover.test.ts tests/sim.test.ts tests/liquid.test.ts` (about 2 minutes).
9. Rest and storm against the approved pair: `npm run fit -- --season <season> --tier draft --format hd --state rest --box 858,99,1964,1205 --name <name> --force`, and the same with `--state landed`.
10. The shake: `npm run sheet -- --shot test --cols 4 --format hd --tier draft --force`; single frames with `--first N --last N` (winter looked at 90, 120, 174 and 204; they lie under `out/work/seq/test-hd-draft/`).
11. Tune in this order: the veil's tint and density, `STORM_PALE_TO` and `STORM_GOLD`, `STORM_GLOW.to`, `STORM_WINDOW` and `driftDipAtMm`, `REST_CLEAR.from`.
12. Measures of this part: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M3,M4,M5,M6 --tier draft`.
- Mind: sprites of the storm must not put large soft flakes over the miniature at rest; `REST_CLEAR` keeps those in front of it small and sharp (at most 3.1 px at hd), and M3 falls without it.
- Mind: the engine's pins hold for `shake.strokeMm` 22 to 25. Outside that, move `ARM_START` in `src/globe/liquid.ts` until "flat" at the landing sits in the middle of 1.25 to 2.2.
- Mind: `stageGridWarm` has no knee; a caller gives it its own small share (`STORM_WARM` 0.4). Only the radial flow coordinate may shear the strands. The flood opens by `f.moment.beat`, never by the globe's height.
- Mind: flakes coming to rest on roof, branches and pond were not built on winter (the liquid's state is needed).
- Mind: `python3 assets-src/flakes/count.py` and `python3 assets-src/flakes/storm.py` count flakes and print the storm's colours; they read winter's approved pair by default and need Pillow, numpy and scipy.

**One more dressing for the glass** (model: `assets-src/frost-winter/`, `src/seasons/winter/frost.ts`)

1. Copy `assets-src/frost-winter/` to `assets-src/<dressing>-<season>/`. In `build.mjs` change `OUT` and the output file names; copy `src/seasons/winter/frost.ts` to the season and change its two map names (lines 13 and 14).
2. Measure the approved images in their own pixels (glass circle centre (1411.5, 653.4), radius 553.9 px) and write `layout.json`: `glass.profile` every 25 to 35 degrees, `glass.plumes`, `glass.tufts`, `glass.crust`, `glass.sprays`, `slab.arms`, `slab.fern`, `slab.drift.profile`, `slab.thrown`.
3. `npm run make -- <dressing>-<season>` (38 s), then `npm run texpack && npm run build`. Draw the maps at 4096 and tag them `.w2048.`.
4. Fit by numbers first: `npm run still -- --season <season> --state rest --camera hero --tier draft --format hd --force`, luminance percentiles of fixed boxes against the approved crop, then one look.
5. Shader constants: `look` at the top of `src/seasons/<season>/frost.ts` (glass: `cover`, `haze`, `thin`, `thick`, `scatter`, `glint`; slab: `light`, `gainKey`, `gainFar`, `gainFront`, `grey`, `wet`, `lumpMm`, `lump`, `far`, `farMips`, `focusMm`).
6. For dust, pollen or leaves in place of ice: change the `SNOW` colours, bring `glint` and `wet` to 0, keep `copyBehind` only if the dressing is translucent.
7. Look from `--camera low` and `--camera surface` as well as `hero`.
8. Measures of this part: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M1,M2,M3,M6 --tier draft`.
- Mind: place lip rime only after the landform is final, from a still of the build. Append new sprays at the end of `glass.sprays`; they draw from their own stream and an insert changes every later one.
- Mind: after every merge of main: `git checkout -- assets-full public/assets`, merge, make, texpack, build, in that order; a stale built file gives a still with the old dressing.
- Mind: for the glass itself, `assets-src/glass/RECIPE.md`, "Making it again for another season", steps 1 to 13; its checks are `node assets-src/glass/sanity.mjs --season <season>` and `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M1 --tier draft`.
- Not in the repo: `out/work/v/box.mjs`, used by the frost builder for luminance percentiles of a box in both pictures.

**One more arrival** (model: `src/seasons/winter/arrival.ts`)

1. Copy `src/seasons/winter/arrival.ts` to `src/seasons/<season>/arrival.ts`.
2. Set `GLASS` and `SLAB`: `start` above `FROST_START` (0.07) so the first texels show at the touch. The growth at `states.landedSeconds` (0.5 s, frame 174) must give what the approved shake image shows (winter: 0.694 on the slab).
3. Set the ring front in the season's `frost.ts`: `look.slab.frontLag`, `frontMm`, `frontEnd`. Keep `frontEnd[1]` a little past the growth at `landedSeconds`. A larger `frontLag` gives a clearer ring and less dressing on the landed still.
4. `TOUCH`: the first sign comes with the foot rolling flat. Read the pose (`rimGapMm(pose)`, `pose.y`), never a frame number.
5. The leaving needs no timing: `kept` follows `changeSwell(...).glow`. For a dressing that should not melt into the light, replace `kept` by `1 - partChange(part, through)`.
6. `npm run build`, then the arrival on a sheet (12 frames across the range): `node tools/sheet.mjs --shot test --first 144 --last 204 --tier draft --look no`.
7. The leaving on a sheet: `node tools/sheet.mjs --shot test --first 84 --last 106 --tier draft --look no`.
8. Read both sheets frame by frame: no crystal appears whole at low opacity, no patch fades in place, the last frame before the span is not whole, nothing recedes. Winter: rime at the rim on 144, half way out on 154, nearly whole on 172.
9. The motion as the critics see it: `npm run sheet -- --motion arrival --shot test --fit --tier review --look no`. The landed still: `npm run still -- --season <season> --state landed --camera hero --tier draft --format hd --force`.
- Mind: the image wins over the ladder: winter's approved shake image shows the slab whole at 0.5 s, so a slower curve left the landed still short. Slower needs a later `states.landedSeconds`, which is `/root`'s.
- Mind: base, glass dressing and surface dressing share one span (`OUTSIDE` in `src/shake/change.ts`). If the dressing's span is set later than the base's, `kept` rises again and the dressing comes back for a few frames.
- Mind: after the landing the arrival reads `moment.sinceLanding`; it holds as long as the landing frame stays 144.

**The light of one more season** (rig, grid, flood)

1. Write `src/seasons/<season>/rig.ts`: key, rim, sky, ground, fill, card. Keep key 0.75 and rim 0.35 unless `/root` rules otherwise. The grid bakes itself from `innerLights`; no make target.
2. `npm run build && npm run fit -- --season <season> --tier draft --state rest --name <name> --force`, then the same with `--state landed`.
3. Fit at rest, one constant at a time, in this order: `inside.through`, `grid.surface`, `grid.flat`, `grid.air`. Then at landed ask the flakes' builder for the veil's shares.
4. The change: fit `flood.inside` for the middle (warm, never white, no ball) and `flood.outside` for the base (not under 16.8). Twelve frames across it: `npm run sheet -- --shot test --first 80 --last 112 --tier draft --force`; consecutive frames: `npm run sheet -- --motion swap --shot test --stride 1 --tier draft`.
5. Single frames at the review tier: `npm run sheet -- --shot test --first 97 --last 97 --format hd --tier review --force` (about two minutes a frame).
6. Measures of this part: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M4,M5 --tier draft`.
- Mind: `ROOM_RIG` is shared by all seasons and the rig is frozen. `stageFlood` reads `uLightEye`: set `f.pose` before `Lighting.update`. A pool for each small light is not built (the grid's cells are 2.9 mm).
- Not in the repo: `lk/trial.sh`, `lk/stats.py`, `lk/boxes.py`, `lk/wmap.py`, `lk/pair.py`, used by the light builder for median luma and the warm bright share of regions and boxes of the fit still beside the approved image.
- Not in the repo: `lk/every.mjs`, `lk/env.py`, `lk/prio.mjs`, `lk/f.sh`, `lk/pre.sh`, used by the light builder for frames 80 to 112 in one page, the whole-frame luminance step of each frame (none above 0.02), single frames at hd, and the checks before a push.

**The liquid's rows for a season**

1. Add or tune the season's rows in `src/particles/engine/kinds.ts`; the liquid (`src/globe/liquid.ts`) and the engine are shared. Then `npm run texpack && npm run build`.
2. `npx tsc --noEmit && npx vitest run` (pins: `tests/engine-storm.test.ts`, `tests/engine-cover.test.ts`, `tests/sim.test.ts`, `tests/liquid.test.ts`).
3. Stills beside the approved pictures: `npm run still -- --season <season> --state landed --camera hero --tier draft --format hd --force` (also `--state rest` and `--state settled`).
4. Motion: `npm run sheet -- --motion strokes --shot test --fit --tier draft` (also `lift`, `setdown`, `landing`, `arrival`).
5. Measures of this part: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M4,M5,M6 --tier draft`.

**The shake path** (nothing is made)

1. The path comes from `makeShake(name)` in `src/shake/table.ts`; the names of every season's shakes are already in the four pin files.
2. `npx vitest run tests/shake-path.test.ts tests/shake-clearance.test.ts tests/shake-change.test.ts tests/shake-looks.test.ts`, then `npx vitest run tests/engine-storm.test.ts tests/engine-cover.test.ts tests/sim.test.ts`.
3. `npm run build`, then `npm run sheet -- --motion lift --shot shake-1 --fit --tier review --look no`, and the same with `strokes`, `setdown`, `landing` (4.5 to 7 minutes each; exit 3 means run it again).
4. The foot at hd: `npm run sheet -- --motion setdown --shot shake-1 --at 0.3 --seconds 0.6 --stride 2 --tier draft --format hd --look no`.
5. A part that changes in the shake: one row in `SPANS` of `src/shake/change.ts` and its name in `SEASONAL_PARTS`; outside the glass it takes `OUTSIDE`.
6. Tell the builders who read the pose (liquid, flakes, slab, dressing): read `moment.globe` and `rimGapMm(moment.globe)`, never a frame number.
- Mind: any move of `shake.strokeMm` or of `whipTurns`, `whipReach`, `overshootDeg`, `rockDecay` needs the whole of `tests/shake-path.test.ts` again. `OUTSIDE` and `BLOOM_RAMP` are tied to the fastest stroke (frame 97.6).
- Mind: the sheet tool refuses a stale `dist/`: `npm run build` after every edit, never while a render runs, one render at a time.
- Not in the repo: the probe that prints every pin's margin over all 19 names, used by the shake builder before a knob is touched; the four pin files give pass or fail only.

**Checks before every push**

1. `npx tsc --noEmit`
2. `npx vitest run` (winter's last state: 419 passed, 4 skipped)
3. `npm run determinism` (4 to 5 minutes; its last line reads `determinism: pass`)
4. The measures for the unit, at draft: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only <the part's measures> --tier draft` (0 red).
5. The stand-ins still: `npm run still -- --season <season> --state rest --camera hero --tier draft --format hd --diag standins --force` (black: the largest pixel value is 0).
6. Commit by path, `git checkout -- assets-full public/assets`, merge `origin/main` (merge, never rebase), make and build again, test again, push.
- Mind: built files under `assets-full/` and `public/assets/` are committed only by `/root`; a builder reports its make targets and `/root` makes them again.
- Mind: do not run the suite from a copy whose `kit` is a symlink; `tests/clock.test.ts` then writes the real `kit/CLOCK.json`.

**How a part is judged**

1. The fit: `npm run fit -- --season <season> --tier draft --box x0,y0,x1,y1 --name <n> --force` draws the build through the approved image's own camera beside the approved crop and prints the mean difference.
2. The still: `npm run still -- --season <season> --state rest --camera hero --tier draft --format hd --force`, with the approved crop beside it.
3. The tiers are `draft` (2 samples a frame), `review` (8) and `final` (24). Looks and fits are made at draft; critics see review; `npm run measure` defaults to final.
4. Only the final tier counts for M3: `npm run measure -- --unit <season> --only M3 --tier final` (about 4 minutes), against `detail.minSharpnessRatio` 0.75.
5. The draft tier reads about 0.10 higher on M3 (0.08 to 0.09 in the three reports that give both: 1.031 against 0.943, 0.9349 against 0.8473, 0.9312 against 0.8478); sample noise counts as detail there.
- Mind: the lens fill runs at the final tier only, so a macro still at draft still shows the bands; judge the macro with `npm run still -- --season <season> --state rest --camera macro --tier final --format hd --force`.
- Mind: final-tier M3 differed by 0.07 between two VMs on the same files (0.8478 against 0.9223) and is not explained; `/root`'s number is the one that counts.

## Decisions made by the Builder

Numbered. What, why, and the date.

1. 2026-11-24. The image limit is a pixel count, not a long edge. `critics.maxImageLongEdgePx` stays as it is, because a picture that wide arrived untouched at 16:9. The harness adds the measured cap of about 1.14 megapixels from `config/image-limit.json` to every picture meant for a model, so a square crop is at most 1067 on a side. Lowering the key to 1072 instead would have halved the detail of every wide comparison image for no gain.
2. 2026-11-24. The listener is given each take after the level chain, never as the tool returned it, and is asked what it hears before it is shown the cue's line. A voice reported in a sound effect counts only if a second call reports it too. Reason: on an almost silent test take the audio evaluation tool gave five different answers to five agents, one of them a spoken sentence.
3. 2026-11-24. Critics, jurors and listeners are started with sandbox isolation and receive their files as attachments, so they share no disk with the Builder and cannot open the repo, the kit or earlier scorecards. An agent without isolation shares this VM.
4. 2026-11-24. The sound tool is called through the saved credential for the ElevenLabs API, because the connector named in the kit is not among the tools of this task. Same service, same account, same plan.

5. 2026-11-24. `vitest` is pinned to 4.1.12, not to the newest release, because 5.x needs Node 22 and the VMs here have Node 20. Every other package is the newest stable release.

6. 2026-11-24. A still places all its samples at one moment, so only the pixel and the lens are sampled. A film or loop frame also spreads its samples over the shutter. Samples are sorted by time, so the stepped simulation only ever runs forward inside one picture.
7. 2026-11-24. The stepped state of a still or a frame is reached by replay from the start of its timeline, with a checkpoint every `sim.checkpointSeconds`. Checkpoints are kept in the page and, in `capture`, stored by the harness server under `out/work/simcache/`, keyed by the engine hash, so a wrong cache can never be read after the code changed.
8. 2026-11-24. Comparison images and crops are placed relative to the globe's circle: measured in each approved image and computed from the camera in each still. Labels `-a` (approved) and `-b` (build) are the ones the `settled` lines in the config use.
9. 2026-11-24. Build and tool agents run at high reasoning effort. Critics, jurors and agents that judge or build what is seen run at the strongest setting available.
10. 2026-11-24. The parts register is created in Phase 0 instead of Phase 2, with every part as a stand-in, because the frame interface of step 14 reports the stand-in list from it.

11. 2026-11-24. `EXT_color_buffer_half_float` is used only as an optional path. The accumulator asks for a 32 bit float target, falls back to a 16 bit one where that is missing, and to 8 bit where neither exists. The three paths were rendered: the 16 bit path is 75.0 dB from the 32 bit one, the 8 bit path 34.5 dB. Captures always record which one they used.
12. 2026-11-24. In G8, `public/assets/index.json` counts as a file written by the build: `npm run build` writes it from the asset folders, so it has neither a row in `ASSETS.md` nor a recipe.
13. 2026-11-24. `npm run determinism` opens four fresh pages, renders its pictures in four different orders with checkpoints off, and then once more with checkpoints written and read back. All of them must be pixel-identical.
14. 2026-11-24. The default grid is 25 bars: holds of 4, 4, 4, 4 and 5 bars and four shakes of one bar, 60.0 s at 100 beats a minute, 3600 frames at 60 a second and 1800 at 30. It is replaced by the music's own map in Phase 5.
15. 2026-11-24. The app is published at the address in `slug` without asking, because the kit names that key for the address and this task says to decide and continue.
16. 2026-11-24. The first deploy was sent with the host's picture check off. The check looked at the spike and refused it for three things that belong to the spike. A measuring scene is what Phase 0 is asked to publish. The check stays on for every deploy from the first real scene.
17. 2026-11-24. The render budget section is written by hand from two measurements, this VM's and a sandbox VM's, because the film will be rendered on sandbox VMs and their draw time is about half. `npm run budget` still writes `out/work/budget/`, and `--write` is not used.


18. 2026-11-24. The glass refracts by moving vertices, not by bending a picture. Everything inside the liquid is drawn in its own pass through one shader chunk that finds, for each vertex, the point on the sphere where the ray from the lens enters so that Snell's law holds with `globe.liquidIor`, and places the vertex along that ray at its true optical path length. The inside is therefore drawn once, sharp, with correct depth, in every sample, and depth of field and motion blur come from the samples like everything else. A picture bent in screen space was the alternative: it loses detail at the limb and cannot hold depth of field through the glass. The shell (reflection of the room, the Fresnel rim, the bubble, the season's dressing) is drawn over it.
19. 2026-11-24. Materials are three.js standard and physical materials patched through one helper, so every part gets the interior projection, the light grid, the dissolve by hashed coverage and the diagnostic views from one place, and a season's folder never touches shader plumbing.

21. 2026-11-24. The mirror image in the surface is the scene drawn again from the camera reflected in the surface's plane, with the same shader code. The small lights inside the globe light the miniature through a coarse grid of light baked on the CPU, so their count costs nothing per pixel.
22. 2026-11-24. One pool of particles sized to the largest season. A particle's kind comes from its index, and at a change the outgoing and incoming kinds are paired by index, so the storm never loses or gains a particle in one frame. The live app steps the same simulation in a worker.
23. 2026-11-24. The cut list is computed in the page (`src/film/cut.ts`) from the config and the audio map, and `npm run cut` writes it out by asking the built page. The film, the checks and the mix therefore read one cut, and it cannot drift from what the page renders.

The Builder builds the engine, the stand-ins, the film, the app and the seasons' skeleton meanwhile and integrates. The briefs are committed under `tools/briefs/`, so a replacement or a later task starts from the same words.
25. 2026-11-24. The npm script lines for every remaining harness command were added before the agents started, so no agent edits `package.json`.
26. 2026-11-24. The engine and the film with the app, which decision 24 kept with the Builder, went to two more sandbox agents at 03:48 and 03:50 (ten build agents, the limit in the config). Reason: the Builder's working memory is compacted every few minutes at this size of task, and each compaction costs a full reading of the kit, so code written here would be written between readings. The Builder now holds the contracts (`src/core/stage.ts`, `src/core/bridge.ts`), verifies every branch through the harness, integrates, runs the rounds, the sound and the statuses. The stage contract was widened first so the two can work apart: the moment carries the time, the beat and the loop's period, the particles reach the stage as one typed source, the stage gives the collider.
