The image wins, as with the frost in decision 47: the icicles lengthen from the swap, under the blur of the strokes, and through the landing, reach nearly their full length by `states.landedSeconds`, and finish, with the drip at their tips, over the next seconds. A winter into winter shake must not show stubs in a clean frame. `src/scene/stage.ts` keeps `arrived` at 0 from `shake.swapWindow.fromBeat` to the landing in a one-season shake as in a two-season one, so the test shake shows what a real arrival shows; the base owns what it does with it.

52. A juror is sent one sentence more than the kit's question: "Send your answer as one message to `/root`." (14:10 UTC, Phase 3, Round 3). `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md` says a juror "receives one image and exactly this text, nothing else". Here a fresh agent's answer reaches the Builder only as a message it sends, so without an address there is no answer to count. The sentence names no picture, no options and no project; the same sentence goes to every juror, on controls and judged images alike. Critics get the matching sentence after their pack's message, as since Round 1.
53. Round 4 judges the finishers' finished work, and rounds from here are about two hours apart (14:35 UTC, Phase 3). The second set of build agents ends at 16:17 UTC, before any answer of a round frozen at 15:30 could reach them, so an earlier freeze would spend one of `quality.lookLockMaxRounds` rounds on work an hour short of done. The freeze is 16:00 UTC. The clock allows it: extra rounds in Phase 3 stop when the time used reaches the boxes of Phases 0 to 3 together, 22 h, and 12.7 h are used, because Phases 0 to 2 took 6.6 h of their 11. A round is not started unless the fixes for the round before it have had about an hour of work; a round whose judged build would differ little from the last one's is not worth its count.

54. Each round's fix list is a new set of jobs, one per part, with one build agent each (written 15:40 UTC, Phase 3, before anyone is started on it). `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md` allows one replacement of an agent per job, and decision 49 spent that replacement on the finishers, whose three hours end at 16:17 UTC; it also said that a later round's fix list for a part is logged as a new job before anyone is started on it. The kit's Phase 3 loop (`kit/08-BUILD-ORDER.md` step 6) runs rounds until the pass bar holds, the count is reached or the clock ends the loop, and step 2 gives the work between rounds to supporting agents, one owner per folder; it does not say who fixes what a later round finds once a builder's hours are spent. Reading taken, the one that keeps the work moving toward the approved images: after each freeze, the open severe and major issues of the rounds judged so far, with the outgoing agent's own list of what is still wrong, are one new job per part (`tree`, `village`, `base`, `frost`, `flakes`, `liquid`, `scene`, `glass`, `light and shake`, `load and checks`), named `round N fixes: <part>`. Each job gets one build agent with a limit of at most `budget.buildAgentMaxHours`, the same file ownership as before, the critics' words verbatim and the newest side by side pictures; the answers of the rounds judged while it works are added per part as they arrive, and when its hours end, what the later rounds found is the next job for that part. Each such job may have its one replacement if its agent runs past its limit or goes silent. A part with nothing open gets no agent. Build agents stay inside `sandbox.maxAgents` less `sandbox.criticSlots` (10), so the round's critics, jurors and render agents can always start. This lowers no threshold and drops no critic; it only says who does the fix work. It is listed in the REVIEW block.

55. The landing may rock by up to 1.3 degrees (ruled 16:13 UTC, Phase 3, when the third set of builders was started; in force once the shake's builder pushes it). `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` and `kit/03-METHOD.md` say of the set down "a small overshoot in tilt that dies inside `shake.landSettleSeconds`" and "It does not bounce into the air", and give the overshoot no number. The 0.3 to 0.7 degree band in `tests/shake-path.test.ts` was the Builder's own. Round 3's motion critic (`scorecards/round-003/R4-2.json`) read the landing as a hover: the globe arrives level and stops, with no weight. So the band's upper bound may rise to 1.3 degrees if the set down and landing sheets at `review` tier then show one edge meeting the slab first and a short damped rock; the rock still dies inside `shake.landSettleSeconds`, which stays at its value (it is not one the Builder may tune), and the foot never leaves the slab after first contact. `shake.tiltDeg` may go from 6 to at most 7.5 (a quarter up, inside AGENTS.md's range) if the shake's builder asks for it. Listed in the REVIEW block.
56. The app's texture set ships as WebP near-lossless decoded to plain RGBA, not as KTX2 (16:16 UTC, Phase 3; changes decision 39). `kit/06-ASSETS.md` asks for the `web` set "in a compressed GPU format with a fallback every target browser reads", and decision 39 named KTX2 with Basis Universal. The loading builder measured it on nine of winter's maps before shipping (`out/work/reports2/load-detail.md`, section 3): UASTC level 2 with zstd is larger on the wire than WebP near-lossless on seven of the nine, by up to 2.8 times, and less exact on the normal and height maps by 8 to 18 dB (36 to 38 dB against 46 to 48); ETC1S gave 20 to 39 dB. What a GPU format buys is texture memory, and G15 reads 129 MB of `assetSets.web.maxTextureMb` 500 with every map held as RGBA, while the bytes fetched are what `host.firstLoadMaxMb` and the visitor's wait are made of (first picture 9.4 MiB of 16). The kit did not foresee its format costing both exactness and weight here, so the reading closest to the approved image is taken: the exact maps. Every map is checked as it is written (no channel of any pixel off by more than 2 of 255). The line of the kit is therefore not met as written; it is in "Open items" and "For Brandon to decide", and in the REVIEW block. If a phone turns out to need the memory when the app is judged in Phase 5, the place for a second coding is `tools/lib/webset.mjs` and one branch in `src/core/textures.ts`.

57. The reference camera is corrected and `hero` follows it in distance (16:38 UTC, Phase 3; closes open item 33). Two circle fits of the approved winter outline agree (the glass builder's, 87 to 99 edge points, rms 1.2 px; the scene builder's, 160 points, rms 0.9 px: centre (1411.5, 653.4), radius 553.9 px in 2816 by 1584), and a render through the old reference camera gave (1407.3, 643.0), radius 549.7. The camera that closes the sphere is 615.3 mm away with its aim 64.0 mm up the axis (pitch 4.5, 16 degrees). So: `REF_RIG` in `tools/lib/refcam.mjs` goes from 619.9 and 62.8 to 615.3 and 64; `cameras.hero.distanceMm` from 616 to 615.3 (the radius was 0.7 px small; inside `framing.heroFillMin`: 139 mm over 172.95 is 0.804); `MOVES.holdAimDropMm` in `src/film/moves.ts` from 4.5 to 3.4, so that the film's holds, which lower the aim from `hero`'s 67.4, show the approved outline (67.4 less 3.4 is 64.0). `hero`'s aim stays 67.4: raised to 68.6 (the corrected reference plus decision 41's 4.6) the shake clearance test fails, and lower it loses the room the lifted glass needs; at rest a `hero` still therefore shows the glass about 27 px of 1584 lower than the approved image, which is decision 41's price and stays listed for Brandon (item 6). Not closable by a camera number, and left open: the approved outline sits 4.2 px right of the axis (a shift of the picture's centre), and the foot's front edge lies about 30 px lower in the build than in the approved image once the sphere is closed, because the glass centre stands 79 mm above the slab against 76 in the picture; `globe.baseHeightMm` 30 would close it and is not taken, because `framing.heroFillMin` would then fail (136 over 172.95 is 0.786) and a threshold is not lowered (decision 40 stands). Tests of framing, film, clearance, loop, cut, lens and config pass (105).

58. A critic's issue that names things no file of its pack shows stays open against the nearest real fault in the crops it names (17:00 UTC, Phase 3, Round 4). R3 part 2 (`kimi_k3`, `scorecards/round-004/R3-2.json`) gave 6 and three majors. Two of them, on `set-D-02.jpg` (the crop sheet of the `base` still), describe "the trees and lamp behind the globe on the right" and "the cobblestone foreground and puddle margin"; that still shows the ledge snow, the icicles and the carved band, and no tree, lamp, cobble or puddle is in any file of the pack. The kit did not foresee an issue about something that is not in the picture. `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md` lets an issue be declined only where it asks to move away from an approved image or a stated choice of Brandon's ("No other severe or major issue may be declined"), so these two are not declined. The reading taken: the answer stays verbatim, its score counts in the merged score, and each of the two issues stays open against what its named crops do show: crops 4 and 6 show the ledge snow reading as grey faceted stone with a plastic speckle (given to the base's builder at 16:58 as a major); crop 5 shows the icicles and the rim band (already a major of the same answer). Each closes when a later round's critic of the same still raises nothing major there. Listed in the REVIEW block.
59. The shake path may wind up sideways in the lift and carry its last swing into the set down (17:11 UTC, Phase 3, Round 4). Both motion critics of Round 4 marked the set down: "base hangs at 1.80 to 2.00 s and lowers straight down without weight". `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` says of the set down "The last stroke carries the globe down". The test `tests/film.test.ts` ("has three parts of the lengths the config gives") held the path to no sideways motion at all in the lift and in the set down; that bound was mine and not the kit's, and it forbade what the kit's own sentence asks. Changed: in the lift and in the set down the sideways offset stays under 0.7 of `shake.strokeMm` (the shake's builder measures 11 to 13 mm against a stroke of 22), and the count of strokes starts from the side the lift ends on, so the path still holds exactly `shake.strokes` strokes inside the strokes part, each still measured against `shake.strokeMm`. The lift still rises without falling back and still reaches `shake.liftMm`; the landing still touches on the landing frame moving down. The kit's lift says "a short dip as if gripped, then up by `shake.liftMm` and tilted back a few degrees, easing" and does not speak of a sideways wind up; the reading taken is that a hand about to shake draws back as it lifts, and that it is judged by R4 like the rest of the path. Listed in the REVIEW block.

60. Round 5 is frozen with one test known to be red, rather than without the village's work or with the cottage lowered (18:02 UTC, Phase 3). On the merged build `tests/engine-cover.test.ts:87` fails: the fresh snow on the lower of the two roof slopes at `settled` reads 0.186 mm against a floor of 0.2 (the other line of the test, the fuller slope above 0.6, passes). Cause, measured by the two owners: the village's builder raised the cottage's walls from 9.4 to 10.8 mm and the roof with them by 1.6 mm so that the apex sits where the approved image has it after the camera correction (decision 57); the pitch is unchanged (42.3 against 41.6 degrees) and the collider is the real roof; the engine's owner found the roof only ever kept what was left after the liquid slowed, which is why 1.6 mm flips it, and has no cure it would keep before the freeze (it has one in work for 19:10: both slopes, with room, over the range of shake paths). The three ways out were: lower the walls again (green, but the apex 13 px under the approved image's: the image wins), leave the village's branch out (green, but a round lost on the chimney's plume, the shrubs, the post caps and the masonry, which three jurors and three critics named), or freeze as it is. Taken: freeze as it is. The floor is not lowered and the test is not touched; the red test makes G2 red in Round 5's machine checks and counts as a severe issue of that round, so Round 5 cannot pass whatever its scores. This differs from the shake path, held back at 17:43 for two red tests: there the readings were half the floor (0.30 against 0.6, 0.56 against 0.6) and what they stand for shows in a picture. Listed in the REVIEW block.

61. The last two rounds of the look lock are frozen at 20:15 and 22:15 UTC (written 18:15 UTC, Phase 3). The count allows rounds 3 to 7 (`quality.lookLockMaxRounds` 5) and the clock allows an extra round to start until the allowance used reaches the sum of the boxes up to Phase 3, 22 h, at about 23:50 UTC; `AGENTS.md`: "While a severe or major issue is open and both the count and the clock allow another round, run it." Round 5's answers arrive from about 19:00 to 19:45; the third set of builders ends at 19:15 and the fourth (decision 54) starts then with a limit of three hours. So Round 6 is frozen at 20:15, after a first fix pass on Round 5's answers; its own answers arrive from about 21:25; Round 7 is frozen at 22:15, at the fourth set's limit, and its answers are in before the clock stops extra rounds. Not chosen: a Round 7 started at about 23:40 with a fifth set of builders. That set would have 75 minutes, of which a new agent needs about 25 to start, and the round would end about an hour and a half past the point where the clock stops extra rounds, time that Phases 4 and 5 lose. This lowers no threshold and drops no round.

62. Round 5's two jury controls are cropped 6 px narrower than in Rounds 3 and 4, and their votes of Round 3 are reused (18:49 UTC, Phase 3). `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md`: the controls are "the approved images, cropped to the globe, its base and the surface around them, resized and encoded exactly like the judged images", and "A control's votes are collected once and reused in later rounds." The pack crops every R2 image to one frame taken from the judged stills; decision 57 moved the hero camera from 616 to 615.3 mm, so the frame is 1078 by 1051 px where it was 1084 by 1050, and the two control files have new bytes. They are the same two approved pictures with the same globe, base and surface. Reading taken: the kit's own line, the votes are reused (10 votes, 7 of them (a) or (b)); asking the jurors again would give the judged stills a second draw of the bar that the kit does not allow. The jurors of Round 5 are the same five models as in Rounds 3 and 4, one of each per judged image, so the judged votes and the control votes come from the same mix.
63. While /root's own push fails, `main` is pushed by an idle agent from a git bundle (19:28 UTC, Phase 3). At 19:25 UTC, after the session was resumed, every push from /root's sandbox failed with "could not read Username for 'https://github.com'" (twice, a minute apart; reads still work). `AGENTS.md` has a failed push logged and tried again at the next commit, and that is done at every commit. But ten builders merge `origin/main` and four render agents draw the freeze from it, so a `main` that stays behind would stop the round. Until the push works again, /root bundles its new commits (`git bundle create ... origin/main..main`) and `gate_runner`, whose sandbox can push, fetches the bundle and pushes it as a plain fast forward: no merge, no rebase, no commit of its own, so author, messages and trailers are /root's, unchanged. Used once, at 19:28 for `92a18c7`. /root's own push worked again a minute later (`7ff842f`); the bundle stays the fallback (`out/work/bin/relay.sh`). Nothing else changes: agents' own branches are fetched as before.
64. `shake.strokeMm` is 24, was 22 (19:56 UTC, Phase 3). `AGENTS.md` lets the Builder tune it by up to a quarter either way, 16.5 to 27.5. Why: Round 5's motion critic read the globe as not moving, so the strokes are as long as the pins allow with room to spare. The shake's closer ran the whole suite with 22, 24, 25, 26, 27 and 27.5 on the path of `ebddaba`: green up to 25; from 26 one test fails, `tests/engine-storm.test.ts` "turns as one flat swirl at the landing" (its `flat` reads 1.53 at 22, 1.35 at 24, 1.27 at 25, 1.19 at 26, against more than 1.25). 24 leaves 0.10 on that pin. Every pin of the shake and every frame rule (4.5 percent of room above the glass, 1 mm at the rim, speed, pull, jerk, the framing for phone and tablet) holds up to 27.5. Written once the path with `awayMm` was on `main` (`355104c`).
65. `shake.strokeMm` is 22 again, was 24 for 7 minutes (20:03 UTC, Phase 3; decision 64 is withdrawn for Round 6, not for good). Why: decision 64's figures were measured on the old liquid engine. With the liquid closer's new engine merged (`d38e0c2`, the cure for the dashes that cross the flow: ladder move 6) and the new lift, 24 fails one pin, `tests/engine-storm.test.ts` "still has flakes through the whole dome at rest" (the high band at rest reads 0.277 against over 0.3). The shake's closer ran the six readings of that file at 22, 22.5, 23, 23.5, 24, 24.5 and 25 on that merge: only 22 (high band 0.346, nothing within 5 % of a pin) and 23 (0.314, green by 5 %) pass. The liquid's closer voted for 22 with its engine in, as the smaller loss for one round. The suite on the merge with 22: 416 passed, 0 failed, 4 skipped. No pin was moved and no test was changed. For Round 7 the liquid's closer makes the high band hold at 24 to 25 with margin; the value goes up again only with a green suite.

66. `shake.strokeMm` is 25, was 22 (21:20 UTC, Phase 3, for Round 7; it takes up decision 64 again, which decision 65 withdrew for Round 6 only). `AGENTS.md` lets the Builder tune it by up to a quarter either way, 16.5 to 27.5. Why: Round 6's motion critic reads the strokes as "a soft rock", and decision 65 held the value back only until the liquid's engine carried it. With the liquid closer's engine `cb51a3e` merged, the whole suite is green on the merged `main` at 24 (419 pass, 21:18) and at 25 (419 pass, 21:23); the shake's closer ran it green at 22, 24, 25, 26 and 27.5 and the liquid's closer read its pins at 22, 23, 24 and 25 (flakes through the dome at rest 0.50, 0.49, 0.47, 0.43 against over 0.3; the landed storm's flatness 1.80, 1.91, 1.83, 1.68 inside 1.25 to 2.2). 25 is the top of what the liquid's closer checked, so it goes no higher. Seen by the shake's closer at 25 beside the approved shake image: the same picture, the tree about 5 percent more veiled, nothing outside the glass moves; the strokes' side travel is 26.3 mm either way (23.1 at 22). No pin moved. Listed for the `REVIEW` block.
67. The sheet "the globe at rest across several seconds" of winter's motion pack starts where the opening's pull back ends (21:27 UTC, Phase 3, from Round 7 on; `tools/pack.mjs`). A conflict the kit did not foresee: `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md` asks for "one sheet of the globe at rest across several seconds", the pack tool took it from the season's first hold, and winter's first hold is the film's `open` shot, whose first two bars are the opening's pull back from the `macro` camera (`kit/01-FILM.md`: "The camera is already moving in frame 0, pulling back"; "The pull back reaches the `hero` camera by the end of the shot's second bar"). Round 6's motion critic was told it saw the globe at rest, saw 3.5 seconds of camera move and marked it severe. The opening's camera is the `film` unit's to judge, and the film's own pack already carries the sheet "the opening pull back". So for the hold that opens the film the rest sheet now runs from the end of its second bar to its last frame (frames 288 to 575, 4.8 seconds, with the drift of a few degrees that `kit/01-FILM.md` asks for); every other hold is drawn whole as before. Nothing in the picture changes. Listed for the `REVIEW` block.

68. **The engine may start with the calm snowfall under way** (21:38 UTC, Phase 3, Round 6's fixes). Round 6's motion critic (part 2) wrote that "at 0.00 s before the shake the liquid contains zero drifting snow particles", while the approved image of winter at rest shows fine flakes in the liquid and `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` says a flake at rest in still liquid "sinks slowly with a slight sideways wander". The engine's start had every winter flake lying, and one of the Builder's own pins in `tests/sim.test.ts` ("starts each season the same way every time, with what rests already lying") held it there: `expect(census(winter).rest).toBe(winter.count)`. The pin is not one of the tests `kit/05-ARCHITECTURE.md` lists and no config threshold; it now reads: what rests plus what drifts is every flake, and what drifts is under 6 % of them. It passes on `main` as it was (none in the air) and with the liquid's change (about 4 % in the air at step 0, level over 120 s without a shake); the same start every time is still pinned by the lines above it. A lower bound (more than 2.5 % in the air) goes in once the liquid's branch is merged.

69. **The flood's own pin holds the light by amounts, not by shape** (22:05 UTC, Phase 3, Round 6's fixes). Round 6's motion critic saw "a blown-out white orb" grow in the middle of the storm. The light's closer made the flood inside the glass wide and low (ten times lower on the line through the lights, the halo as it was from 25 mm out) and said plainly that it rewrote its own test for it: `tests/light-flood.test.ts` held the flood's shape (under 0.04 of the middle at 45 % of the radius, under 0.004 at 95 %), which is the bright core the critic named; it now holds the light at those places in absolute amounts (under 0.06 and 0.006; they read 0.033 and 0.0058) and adds that the middle stays under 0.2 and under 6 times the halo. The pin is the closer's own, not one of the tests `kit/05-ARCHITECTURE.md` lists and no config threshold; the old lines pinned the fault, so the rewrite is accepted. M4 and M5, which judge what the change shows, are untouched and green (0.986 and 0.9996 at draft).

70. **No Phase 4 agent started before Phase 3's gate closed** (decided 22:35 UTC during Round 7, logged 23:58 UTC, Phase 3, the close). Round 7 of the look lock took about two hours from its freeze to its last answer, and ten build slots stood idle through it. Starting the seasons' builders during that wait would have saved that time, but they build from "Look lock", which is written at Phase 3's end from what its last round showed, and the kit's order has Phase 4 start after Phase 3's gate. So the wait was used to prepare only: each season's folder split into one file per part, the briefs, the fit tool's reference camera for a season. Nothing of that changed what a still shows.

71. **Phase 4's split: three builders for each season, one for the drawing of all seven new particle kinds, ten in all; the sound agent takes the first build slot that frees** (prepared 22:35 to 22:47 UTC during Round 7, logged 23:58 UTC, for Phase 4). The kit's table asks for four agents for each season and one for sound, thirteen. `AGENTS.md` and `kit/08-BUILD-ORDER.md` let build agents hold at most `sandbox.maxAgents` less `sandbox.criticSlots`, ten, and the kit says "Change the split if the work calls for it, and log it". The split:
    - `<season>_base`: the base, what lies on the surface, the arrival outside the glass (`assets-src/base-<season>/`, `assets-src/slab-<season>/`, `base.ts`, `slab.ts`, `arrival.ts` of `src/seasons/<season>/`).
    - `<season>_mini`: the miniature with its small lights, its distance field and its pond (`assets-src/village-<season>/`, `assets-src/tree-<season>/`, `miniature.ts`, `mini/`).
    - `<season>_air`: the glass dressing, the veil, the season's light (rig and backdrop), the storm's tuning, and the rows of its kinds in `src/particles/engine/kinds.ts` (`assets-src/dressing-<season>/`, `assets-src/refrig/<season>.json`, `glass.ts`, `veil.ts`, `rig.ts`, `backdrop.ts`).
    - `kinds_builder`: the drawing of petal, mote, firefly, seed, leaf, ember and bat (`src/particles/draw/`, `assets-src/kinds/`, `src/particles/engine/engine.ts`). One builder for all seven, because they share one shader, one table of looks and one draw call; three builders in those files would have merged against each other all phase.
    - The surface dressing went from the kit's third agent to the base's builder, because it lies around the base and is lit and judged with it; the arrival's parts are built by whoever owns the thing that arrives, and the base's builder owns `arrival.ts`.
    - Sound: one agent, in this workspace and not in a sandbox VM, because the sound tool is reached through the saved credential and the listener is a tool of this workspace. It is the eleventh builder, so it starts when the first of the ten is done.
    - `src/seasons/<season>/index.ts`, `config/showcase.json`, `parts/PARTS.json`, the shared systems and winter stay the Builder's.

72. **Winter ends on the round count; the build kept is Round 7's, and Round 7 is named its best round** (23:34 UTC, Phase 3, the close). Round 7 was the fifth round of the look lock, the last `quality.lookLockMaxRounds` allows, and it does not pass: R1 7, R3 5, R4 4, the jury 4 of 15 against the control's 7 of 10. kit/07 keeps "the build from the unit's best judged round, plus later fixes only while the machine checks and measures stay green". The five rounds read, as R1, R3, R4 and the jury's count of 15: Round 3: 4, 3, 3, 4. Round 4: 5, 4, 5, 4. Round 5: 5, 6, 3, 3. Round 6: 5, 5, 5, 5, with M3 red. Round 7: 7, 5, 4, 4, with every check and every measure green. No round leads on every role. Round 7 is named best because it is the only round whose likeness score left 5, its craft score is no lower than Round 6's, and of the last two it is the one with every measure green. Its motion score of 4 is the lower of two parts: the critic with the ten sheets of consecutive frames gave 7 and wrote "The strokes do have clear reversals"; the critic with the two overview sheets gave 4 and wrote "the globe never moves during the shake", from a sheet that holds two frames of the shake. Both answers stand as written, and both severe issues are open items. Whichever of Rounds 4 to 7 were named best, the build kept would be the same commit, `4f10d97`: it is each earlier round's build plus the later fixes, with the gate and the measures green. No threshold was changed and no critic was dropped. Listed in the REVIEW block.

73. **Phase 3's gate was closed in two parts: the whole gate on a clean clone of the judged build, then the checks that read the log and the stand-ins after winter was marked finished** (23:58 UTC, Phase 3, the close). Step 10 of Phase 3 in `kit/08-BUILD-ORDER.md` asks for the gate after the unit is marked `finished`. Round 7's gate ran before that, on a clean clone of `4f10d97` by `gate_runner`: 15 of 15 green (`scorecards/round-007/gate-phase-3.json`), but without the unit named and with no unit finished, so its G13 covered no part. After winter was marked `finished` (`1ee1285`), G13 was run in this workspace with the unit named: green, it covers winter's 17 of 54 parts, and the stand-in view of 69 of 69 judged stills and frames shows no pixel of a covered part (23:21 to 23:41 UTC). Then G6, G9, G13 and G15 were run again on the closing state of the log and the tracked files: all green (`scorecards/round-007/gate-phase-3-close.json` holds the merged 15 lines). The other eleven lines are those of the clean clone: no file under `src/`, `public/`, `config/`, `tools/`, `tests/`, `assets-src/` or `package.json` differs between `4f10d97` and the closing commit (`git diff --stat` is empty), so they were not run a second time here. Why not the whole gate again before closing: it takes about 70 minutes with G13 covering a unit, and Phase 3 is four hours past its box. As a confirmation, `gate_runner` was asked at 23:35 UTC to run the whole gate once more on a fresh clean clone of `1ee1285`; its result is logged in the session log when it arrives, and a red line there reopens the check as an open item at once.

74. **Each new season gets an allowance of 18 MiB in the web pack, split by part, and the builders were told in writing** (01:03 UTC, Phase 4). `host.maxShippedMb` holds all of `dist/` to 95 MB (G16, Phase 5). Winter's web set is 18.0 MiB and the shared set 7.3 MiB; with every first version merged at 00:56 the pack stood at 83.8 MiB (spring 25.5, summer 22.3, fall 10.9), which with the code and the sound pack would be over the limit before any tuning. So: base and slab files together 6.5 MiB, miniature (village and tree files) 9.5 MiB, glass dressing and veil files 2 MiB, per season, as winter has. Sent to the nine season builders at 00:57 with where each stood; they cut what the judged cameras cannot see first. The limit itself is not touched. The triangle and draw call limits of G15 are measured on the merged build and sent the same way.

75. **The first rounds of spring and summer start together on one judged commit; fall's first round waits for free critic slots** (01:35 UTC, Phase 4). kit/08 sends a season to its first round "as soon as nothing its stills show is a stand-in". At 01:28 that held for spring and for summer (the stand-in view of each is black all over), 77 minutes into the phase and 90 minutes before the builders' limit, so both rounds were counted at once on `fe1072c`: Round 8 on spring, Round 9 on summer. Critics, jurors and render agents share `sandbox.criticSlots` 6. Round 8's renders take three (the gate, the pack, the motion pack); Round 9's take two (one new render agent for the pack, and one that draws the motion pack and then runs the gate), which leaves one slot for the first critics. Fall's stand-in view is checked the same way; its first round (Round 10) is frozen as soon as three slots are free with no critic of a running round waiting, and at the latest at the builders' limit of 03:02 UTC, on the newest merged build. The builders go on to their limit; what the critics say reaches fresh fix agents after it, and a season's second round follows while the count and the clock allow.

76. **Fall's first round starts now, one part at a time, as slots free up** (02:14 UTC, Phase 4; this replaces the waiting rule of decision 75). AGENTS.md's clock rule 3 sends a unit to its first round as soon as none of it is a stand-in, and fall's stand-in view was black all over at 01:39. At 02:01 and 02:04 the two motion agents reported that a season's arrival sheet is about 90 frames at 51 to 54 s a frame on their VMs (winter's frames were lighter), so a motion pack takes about 100 minutes, not 35: waiting for three free slots would have put fall's first answers after 05:00. Round 10 is therefore counted now on a freeze commit made from the newest merge, its motion pack goes to a new render agent in the one free slot, its still pack goes to the first pack agent that finishes and has no critic of Rounds 8 or 9 waiting behind it, and its gate goes to `gate_runner` after the gates of Rounds 8 and 9. The limits of Round 8's and Round 9's motion packs were moved from 02:46 to 03:35 (nothing is cut from a pack), and Round 9's gate was taken off `motion_renderer_b` and goes to `gate_runner` after Round 8's, on the same clean clone of the same commit. Never more than six critics, jurors and render agents work at once.

77. **The second set of build agents, and the split it works in** (02:42 UTC, Phase 4). The ten builders of the first set reach `budget.buildAgentMaxHours` (3 h, started 00:11) at 03:11, so each hands over by 03:02 with a `REPORT.md` and a `HANDOVER.md` on its looks branch, and fresh agents take the files. A successor starts as soon as the agent before it has handed over (the first two, `spring_mini2` and `fall_mini2`, at 02:42), the rest at 03:03; none works past three hours. The split changes, as kit/08 allows ("Change the split if the work calls for it, and log it"), because two more agents have to fit inside the ten build slots (`sandbox.maxAgents` 16 less `sandbox.criticSlots` 6): the sound agent, which kit/08 wants working "while the seasons are built" and which could not start while all ten slots built seasons, and one agent on the changes. So: a miniature agent and an air agent for each season (`spring_mini2`, `spring_air2`, `summer_mini2`, `summer_air2`, `fall_mini2`, `fall_air2`), one agent for the three bases and slabs (`bases2`; the bases are the furthest along of the three kinds of part), one for the drawing of the new particle kinds (`kinds2`), `change_builder` and `sound_maker`. The three seasons' `arrival.ts` go from the base builders to `change_builder`, because kit/08 puts "Finish each season's arrival" in the `change` unit and one file has one owner. `change_builder` starts the code of the four changes now, beside the seasons' rounds, not after them: the clock leaves no room after the seasons' last rounds (they may start until about 09:15, the box ends about 09:38), and kit/08 gives the unit `src/shake/` and "one agent for each change if it helps". The unit `change` itself still goes to its rounds only once the three seasons are finished or at their limits, or at the end of the box on whatever the seasons are, as kit/08 says. What this costs: the base of each season has a third of an agent in place of a whole one.

78. **Sound: the tool's account has no credits left, so what is not made yet stays silent** (02:54 UTC, Phase 4). At 02:52 the sound agent reported from the account's own API (`GET /v1/user/subscription`, its read of 02:51; stated by the agent, not read again by the Builder): tier `creator`, 130996 of 131000 credits used, 4 left, no extension allowed, reset 2026-12-23 20:08 UTC. Its first take (`shake-spring`, 2.6 s) came back HTTP 401 `quota_exceeded` ("You have 4 credits remaining, while 29 credits are required"). No take was counted and nothing was spent. The counter stood at 48421 at 04:02 UTC on Nov 24 and this build's sound calls cost about 1100 in all, so about 82500 went on other work on the account since. The cheapest effect costs 6 and music about 13.8 a second: nothing fits. No other tool here makes sound effects or music (tool record, Phase 0) and there is no other credential. AGENTS.md: "If no sound tool can be called at all, log it, build everything silent, and say so in the first line of the `REVIEW` block: Brandon then supplies the audio himself." So: the four kept sounds stay in the app (`glass-clink`, `ui-sound-on`, `ui-tick`, `lift`); the thirteen cues with no take yet and both candidate soundtracks are not made (open item 63) and their moments are silent; the sound agent writes every prompt, the composition plan for `one-piece` if that call costs nothing, the four track prompts and a recipe that a fresh agent or Brandon can run the moment there are credits, runs the mix and the sound checks on what exists, then reads the counter every 30 minutes until its limit and starts the takes in the order of its brief if it has room; after that the counter is read again at each hand-over of the build agents and before the `REVIEW` block. About 10000 credits cover what is left (about 1500 for 13 effects at 3 takes; about 2500 for `one-piece` at 3 takes of 60 s; 4000 to 6000 for `four-tracks`). No picture, round or gate waits on this.

79. **The sound agent's place goes to a second base agent** (03:01 UTC, Phase 4). With no credits on the sound tool's account (decision 78) the sound agent has only its free work left (prompts, plan, recipe, mix and checks on what exists); it reports and ends, and the Builder reads the counter with every status (`out/work/bin/credits.sh`; the Builder's own read at 02:57:12 UTC: 130996 of 131000 used, 4 left). Its place among the ten build agents goes to the bases, which carry red measures in every season (M3 at rest: spring's base 0.648, summer's 0.604 against 0.75) and many of the critics' issues: `bases2` (started 03:00, limit 05:55) takes spring's and summer's bases and slabs, and `fall_base2` takes fall's base and slab as soon as the sound agent has ended (about 03:30; fall's first answers arrive at about 04:10 and fall freezes last, at 05:30). Until then nobody changes fall's base and slab. This changes decision 77 in that one point (there `bases2` held all three). A fresh sound agent starts from `audio/RECIPE.md` the moment the counter has room, in whatever place is free then.

80. **The sound agent's files reach `main` by copy, with the account's billing fields left out** (03:05 UTC, Phase 4). The sound agent ended at 03:03 with no sound made (decision 78) and left on `agent/p4-sound` (`523ee9d`): `audio/RECIPE.md` (for a fresh agent: every call, all 13 cue texts and 4 track prompts, the judging steps, the chain, the commands in order, a cost table of about 3300 credits for one take of everything and about 9900 for three), `audio/prompts.json` (every text, state "not made: no credits"), `audio/plans/one-piece.json` (the composition plan, five sections of 12000 ms; the plan call cost nothing), `audio/QUESTIONS.md`, `audio/lines.json`, `audio/run/*.mjs` (their path after an HTTP 200 is untested) and `audio/evidence/`. Its two saved answers of `GET /v1/user/subscription` hold the account's next invoice amount, which does not belong in a public repo. So the branch is not merged: its `audio/**` is copied onto `main` with those two files reduced to the counter fields, the merge helper leaves the branch out, and the branch is deleted at the remote once a push works again. Its mixes with no music in them were removed by the agent ("a file named after a candidate with no music in it is not a candidate"): until music exists the films are silent, and the app plays its four kept sounds. Its checks: `npm run soundcheck` 7 pass, 1 open (A2, the known item); `tests/audiomap` and `tests/mix` 38 passed.

81. **A per-season gain on the background lights** (03:12 UTC, Phase 4). Round 9's R1 #7 has summer's out-of-focus lights wrong by place: the approved image has a bright warm cluster at the upper right and a mostly dark left, ours had two dim discs at the right and a column of large orange ones along the left. A season chooses only the discs' colours by level, and the same levels stand left and right, so no file of summer's can move brightness from one side to the other. On `summer_air2`'s exact lines I added one opening to two shared files: in `src/seasons/kit.ts` the kit's `backdrop` may carry `gain?: (at) => number`, and in `src/scene/backdrop.ts` `fill()` multiplies each light's energy by it. Where a season gives none the factor is 1, so winter, spring and fall draw the same numbers as before, and the room lights read the same arrays, so the reflections follow. I read this as a season's own data reaching the shared backdrop, the reading closest to the approved image, and not as a rework of the shared system inside a season's round: the places, sizes and look of the lights are untouched. `tsc` clean, vitest 432 passed.

82. **The arrivals stay with the change builder in the third set of build agents** (03:56 UTC, Phase 4). The change builder offered to hand the three files `src/seasons/{spring,summer,fall}/arrival.ts` to the seasons' builders so that each arrival is drawn by the agent that owns the miniature it reveals. All ten build places are taken (`sandbox.maxAgents` less `sandbox.criticSlots`), so an eleventh builder cannot start, and giving the arrivals to the three air builders would split one staged event (the storm dying, then each row of the kit's arrival table in turn) across two owners per season while the air builders still carry the veil, the light and open measures. Chosen: one owner for all four changes and all three arrivals, as kit/08 writes the `change` unit; where a row of the arrival table needs a line in a season's `mini/**`, `veil.ts` or `slab.ts`, the change builder asks that file's owner by message and reports what was agreed. The three air builders were told at 03:55 that their veil must hold its storm density through the landing and thin over `shake.spinDownSeconds`, which is the part of the arrival that lives in their files. The change builder writes a `HANDOVER.md` on its looks branch before its limit so its successor starts from the newest sheets.

## Declined issues

Round 4, R4 parts 1 and 2 (`gpt_6_2_sol`, `claude_opus_5_5`), the fix asked in their major on the tabletop frost: "Keep the existing winter snow fixed in table space through the shake, and let the landing push a few nearby grains outward rather than erasing and repainting the whole patch" and "keep the table frost fixed through the lift, the landing and the settle". Declined as asked, because it moves away from a stated choice of Brandon's: `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` section 6 has the dressing change "at the fastest moment of the strokes, under motion blur, inside a short bloom of the new season's light", and gives winter's arrival as "A ring of frost bursts outward on the surface from the foot of the base" on the landing; the test shake is winter into winter by `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md`, so the frost that leaves and the frost that arrives are the same. What the two critics saw is not declined and stays open as a major with the frost's builder: the frost leaves in a frame that is too clear and comes back "like a mask" and as "a frost decal scaling and resetting"; it has to leave under the blur and the bloom and arrive as growth.
2. Round 4, R3 part 1 (`gemini_4_carbon`), half of its major on the glass: its fix asks to "refract the interior through a liquid volume so peripheral forms magnify and curve near the rim", which is more than the approved images show. Declined in that half only: in the approved winter images the cottage and the tree stand almost undistorted to within a few millimetres of the rim, and the approved image wins. The other half is not declined and stays open with the glass's builder: the rim zone where the picture does compress and darken in the approved image, and the highlight whose edge is razor sharp.

Not an issue in the kit's sense (jurors give a share, not issues) and written here so it is not chased: the framing is the approved images' own composition and decision 41's reference camera. The jury's share stays as counted.

Declined as asked, because it moves away from a stated choice of Brandon's, the shake as the change, as `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` section 6 spells it out: the base, the dressing and the backdrop "change at the fastest moment of the strokes, under motion blur, inside a short bloom of the new season's light pouring out of the globe". The critic judged a test shake that stays winter, which the finished globe never does, so the bloom had nothing to carry. What the critic saw is not declined and stays open with the light's, the shake's and the frost's closers: "the frosted storm globe suddenly flares warm orange and reads as smooth unfrosted glass, the scattered snow on the surface vanishes, then everything snaps back to cold and frosted ~0.2 s later". The bloom must read as light pouring out of the globe with a rise and a fall the eye can follow, the glass must keep its frost inside it, and nothing may snap.

## Open items

Severe and major issues still open when a limit was reached: the unit, the still, the critic's words, what was tried, what to try next.

1. Sound, `amb-winter`: no take fits after the three the cue is allowed. Its line is "wind and distant bells", looped and very quiet. Take 1 (asked for as very quiet, with the loop setting) and take 2 (asked for at full strength, with the loop setting) came back as a rumble under 60 Hz with nothing above it. Take 3 (no loop setting) is a gust of wind, a whoosh, then sleigh bells jingling close by for ten seconds: the listener's words are "the bells are loud and upfront rather than distant" and "a distinct, magical-sounding whoosh". Winter therefore has no ambience in the app and in the mixes. What to try next, when a review round buys new takes: one steady bed of wind with no named instrument up close, the bells as "far away and sparse", no loop setting, 20 seconds, and let the chain make the loop.

2. The particle engine at winter's full pool of 102000 runs the storm at about half real time on one core of this workspace (1.83 to 1.93 s of work for one simulated second, measured in Node by `particles_builder`). The live app therefore needs the worker and a lower `particleFraction` on slow machines, or the storm plays slowly. Carried to Phase 3, where the winter simulation is finished and measured in a browser.
3. The liquid's field fills the whole sphere and treats the ground only as a collider, so flow that points down presses particles into a low layer: at the winter landing about 37 percent of the flakes afloat are within 3 mm of the ground, and at summer rest about 12 percent of the motes, against 3.7 percent for an even spread. The fix named by `particles_builder` is a field that slides along the ground. Carried to Phase 3.
4. The loop's storm is closed paths driven by one run of the liquid, not the engine's storm: it settles faster than the film's and turns about the upright axis only. In winter 1 to 3 of 102000 particles can step up to 3.8 mm near the tip of the cone. Carried to Phase 5, where the card loop is judged.

5. The live app's simulation runs on the main thread. In this workspace's software graphics a shake takes 10 to 15 s of wall time for about 3 simulated seconds at 2 to 5 frames a second, which says little about a real graphics card; the simulation alone costs about 1.9 s for one simulated second of winter's storm at the full pool. The worker path is to be proven in a browser and wired. Carried to Phase 3.
6. Summer at rest through the stage's drawing is a white-out: 9000 seeds of 1.4 to 2.6 mm and 40000 motes all stay afloat. The winter storm at the full pool also fills the glass to plain white in the middle of a shake. The sizes and sink speeds are stand-in numbers in `src/particles/engine/kinds.ts`. Summer's is being fixed now so the blockout's summer frames show the globe; the storm's density is settled in the look lock, Phase 3.
7. In `auto` the quality governor never stepped down at 2 frames a second: it waits for `live.probeFrames` frames between two rests, which a slow machine never reaches. To be fixed by `film_app_builder` so that a slow machine steps down by time.
8. With reduced motion, and on a jump from the menu, the particles change in one frame at the end of the dissolve. A cross-fade needs the dissolve's progress from the session. Carried to Phase 5 (`globe`).

   - 06:25 update to item 6: summer's seeds are now 0.5 to 1.1 mm and the scene shows through at rest. Reduced, not closed: all 9000 seeds and 40000 motes stay afloat, so summer is the busiest season at rest, and the winter storm still fills the glass white in the middle of a live shake. Carried to Phase 4 (`summer`) and Phase 3 (`winter`), where the particle looks are judged.


9. Sound, `land-winter`: no take fits after the three the cue is allowed. Its line is "A heavy glass globe set down on stone, heavy and short, then ice crackling outward". Take 1: a faint thud and no ice (rejected by the numbers). Take 2: one light bright clink, no crackle. Take 3 (asked as two sounds in a row, 3 s): two hollow knocks like a small thing dropped on wood, nothing above 2 kHz, no ice. The tool gives one familiar event for a short prompt and does not put two events in a row. The winter landing plays without this cue in the film and in the app. What to try if a review round names it: one real event that holds both, such as a heavy glass jar set down hard onto crushed ice, asked at 3 s and cut by the chain.

Checked here in headless Chrome on the built page at 960 by 540: the live page runs one module worker with its client, 0 fallbacks, and `?sim=main` runs none (`out/work/workercheck.mjs`). The speed of the engine itself stays open under item 2.

The aim lies on the axis and the kit's check keeps every corner within 0.85 of the radius, so the frame cannot simply pan or widen: by the arithmetic the cottage (21 mm wide at 17 mm left of the axis) and the foot of the tree span about 51 mm and the widest frame the check allows shows about 52 to 55 mm of the inside. Fix under way: `engine_engineer_2` chooses aim, distance, yaw and lens from rendered candidates inside the check; if none holds the whole cottage, the best one is kept and the rest is carried.

22. From the film and app engineer's final report, 2026-11-24 08:03 UTC, carried to Phase 5 (the whole globe). The pause seen twice at 07:02 (the main thread blocked 6.5 s at 2.3 s after the first picture) has no known cause and no fix; it was not seen in 39 runs since, 6 of them on the final commits. An untested guess is a stall or a lost context in the graphics process; the app does not handle a lost WebGL context. To do: handle a lost context (restore, or the poster with a clear line), and watch for the pause on the published app.

23. Same report, carried to Phase 5. On a 60 Hz screen the governor takes a level back only at twice the `minFps` of the level above (80 or 100 frames a second), which a 60 Hz clock cannot show, so a level dropped once never comes back in that visit. A possible fix: count a window as fast when nearly every frame holds the median and the median is at least the `minFps` of the level above, still once per level.

24. Same report, carried to Phase 5. The phone's landscape framing assumes a 60 px bar until the controls are measured, and was not rendered. The ridge's room in the spring move was proven by geometry and test; round 2's sheets show it in pictures since the thinning.

30. Until Phase 3 step 9 a clean clone of `main` builds an app that does not open (11:40 UTC, Phase 3). At that cost film B at hd is 17 h at `draft` and 118 h at `final` for one render process here; a sandbox VM draws a sample in a little over half the time, so about 60 VM-hours at `final`. Owed before Phase 5 plans its renders: (a) where the time goes, one `--diag` or timing run per pass (the lens, the frost's blur of what is behind it, the light grid, the shadow, the mirror image), and a cut of whatever costs most that changes no pixel a judge can see; (b) the same measurement on a sandbox VM with the real scene; (c) the plan of ranges across VMs inside `sandbox.maxAgents`. The live app's frame rate on a real graphics card is a separate question (G15 and the `live.quality` levels) and is measured in Phase 4's probe.

34. Triangles at the `low` level (14:03 UTC, Phase 3). G15 passes with 578302 of `perf.maxTriangles.low` 600000 a sample through the app's path after the 14:00 merges (592138 before them). Every part that grows eats that room about 1.6 times over, because shadow casters are drawn twice. Every finisher was told and reports its part's triangles; G15 is run again at every freeze of `main`. If it goes red: lighter meshes at the low level first, and `live.quality.low.particleFraction`, which the Builder may tune, second.

  - 14:31 UTC, after the merges of 14:20: `low` draws 601727 triangles a sample, 1727 over `perf.maxTriangles.low`; medium 948200 of 1100000; high 1111400 of 1800000. Visible meshes hold 401281, 462481 and 544081; the rest is the casters' second draw. So G15 would be red on main now. `load_finisher` was given the cure at 14:33: lighter meshes for `low` (and `medium` if needed) made by meshoptimizer's simplifier at pack time, picked by the loader by quality level, with targets of 480000 and 900000 drawn. If that is not in by the freeze of Round 4, `live.quality.low.particleFraction` goes from 0.3 to 0.25 for that round (each 0.1 of it is about 20000 triangles) and is put back when the lighter meshes land.

35. The `web` texture set is not in a compressed GPU format (16:16 UTC, Phase 3; decision 56). `kit/06-ASSETS.md` line 71 asks for one "with a fallback every target browser reads". It ships as WebP near-lossless, held as RGBA on the GPU: measured smaller on the wire on seven of nine maps and 8 to 18 dB more exact on the data maps than UASTC, with texture memory at 129 of 500 MB. Open until Brandon rules or Phase 5's phone pass shows a need.

37. Sound, `shake-winter`: no take fits after the three the cue is allowed (20:31 UTC, Phase 3). Its line is "Liquid sloshing and churning inside glass, with snow hiss and tiny bells for winter". Take 1: a spoon stirring a glass. Take 2: ice cubes or dice shaken in a container with liquid sloshing in glass, no hiss and no bells. Take 3 (water in a sealed bottle shaken six times with a strap of small sleigh bells): small sleigh bells shaken four times in open air, 59 % of the energy above 8 kHz and nothing under 1 kHz, no water. The tool gives one of the two materials a prompt names, never both. So the winter shake is silent, as the winter hold (`amb-winter`) and the crackle of the landing (item 9) are. What to try next, in a review round that buys takes: ask for the water alone and the bells alone as two takes and let the chain lay them together, which the kit's one file per cue does not forbid but its take count does today.

Its fix: "make the surface fern frost thin, feathery, and semi-translucent so it grows smoothly out of the icy table". Its lump shading did not work ("the heap is still flat white"). What to try next: no rung left on the ladder that ladder move 13 put the slab frost on ("An arrival looks like a wipe", rung 3, its last). The most promising untried idea: the ladder "A still does not match its approved image" was never climbed for this part past its rung 2; its rung 3 (project the approved image onto the slab and correct from the difference) and rung 4 (new material pictures of the fern and the heap with the approved image as the reference), with translucent branches, dark gaps and wet highlights between them. 40. **The storm half a second after the landing is drawn as ribbons and a smear, not as flakes.** (Round 7, R1, severe; `set-B-01-a.jpg`, the pair of the approved `winter-shake.png` and winter in the landed state from `hero`, hd, final. Also Round 7, R4 part 1, major; `set-D-02.jpg`, the second sheet of the first second after the landing, test shake, review tier.) R1: "The candidate reads as a tight circular vortex made of broad continuous white ribbons. The target mixes finer broken streaks and individually lit flecks at several depths, with a softer snow cloud near the floor. The intended extra occlusion is not the problem." R4 part 1: "The storm clearly outlasts the glass motion, but the brightest snow remains a broad smear across the lower glass as the tree reappears. The end reads more as the mist thinning than distinct flakes slowing and settling." One juror on the landed still: "The snow forms a nearly perfect spiral around the cabin, like a particle effect arranged for a product render." Its own report: "The low drift at the landing is smooth swept ribbons; the approved's is fluffy short fuzzy fibres at mixed angles", "The ring repetition is cured, the silk look is not", and more fine strands with more warp "stay long ribbons". What to try next: rung 5, the last, of "The particles look like confetti or a filter" (raise the count toward `particles.tuneMax`), and with it the closer's untried technique, in its words: "short tufts with their own turn, drawn as particles lying in the drift, or a break of the strand along the flow". "The wood and tabletop flash orange as the large winter snow patches vanish, then the patches visibly grow back from the base after touchdown. Its report: the arrival "is a ring front now, but whole by about frame 176; no fine grain or spicules lead it"; "The leaving is seen on frames 88 to 96 only"; a slower curve "left the landed still short of frost; slower needs a later `states.landedSeconds`". `base_closer` did not look at the base in motion: "the icicles shed in the shake and grow back after the landing". `light_closer` (`agent/p3-light` `4a0a414`, the flood in `0d97a01`) reshaped the bloom that the critic reads as the orange flash. 45. **The liquid does not bend or enlarge the inside near the rim.** (Round 7, R3 part 1, major; `set-A-02.jpg`, native size crops of winter in the rest state from `hero`, hd, final, crop 2 the glass at its rim; and `set-C-02.jpg`, `set-C-03.jpg`, crops of the interior still.) "the rim has two edges, so the wall has thickness, but the cottage, fence, and tree stay straight and the same size up to the glass. The liquid does not bend or enlarge them. The closer rim crops in set-C-02.jpg and set-C-03.jpg show the same thing". Its fix: "enlarge and bend the interior as it nears the inner wall, the way water in a sphere would". Also Round 7, R3 part 2, major (`set-F-01.jpg`, winter in the settled state from `hero`, hd, final, resized to fit): "the globe has an air-bubble ring at its crown as if filled with water, yet the cottage, tree, and background inside are not bent or enlarged by liquid at all, and the background bokeh lights vanish behind a flat dark backdrop inside the rim". Its fix: "fill the inner cavity of the sphere with a refractive liquid volume bridged to the inner glass wall so the miniature magnifies and bends toward the rim and background bokeh refracts through the globe". One juror: "a water-filled globe lacking realistic liquid refraction". Tried: ladder moves 5 and 10 ("The glass reads as a plain ball", rungs 3 and 4). `glass_closer` built rung 4, in the build since `8227de0` and judged in Rounds 6 and 7: "the rim zone from share 0.92 is read from a copy of the inside, squeezed toward the rim; at rest it moves 1237 pixels by more than 2 (largest 42); the approved image shows no more, so it is tuned no further. Refraction view: the miniature is 352 by 511 px with the lens, 306 by 455 px without." A bent edge "built twice gave a dark band at the hero camera and stripes at the base camera". The fix as asked is a candidate to decline in the half that asks more than the approved images show (Declined issues 2; `r7-ladder.md`). What to try next: no rung left. The untried ideas: the same squeeze made to show at the `interior` camera, where the critic's closer crops are, held to what the approved image shows at `hero`; and the discs behind the globe seen through the glass, which is rung 2 of the same ladder ("Add the through view of the room, small and upside down, behind the miniature") and which no report says was fitted. Against it: in `references/approved/winter-rest.png`, as the scribe reads it, no disc is seen through the globe either, so that half is a candidate to decline (`r7-ladder.md`). `[shared: glass]`

46. **The frost on the glass is combed and stencilled.** (Round 7, R1, major; `set-A-02-a.jpg`, crops of the rest pair; "also in set B".) The last sentence of R1's issue on the glass: "The frost is coarser and more regularly combed along the left edge, with some separate stenciled tufts near the floor, instead of dissolving into the thickness of the glass." Its fix: "blend finer uneven feather clusters into the shell". R3 part 1 names the same as a minor (`set-A-02.jpg`): "the ferns repeat along the curve like a flat brush, with little thickness of their own". Also Round 7, R3 part 2, major (`set-F-03.jpg`, crops of the settled still, crop 9): "spiky white frost cards stick straight up out of the interior snow bank with hard cutout edges". Its fix: "replace the upright interior frost cards with fine crystalline rime blended into the snow and glass". Tried: `frost_closer` (`agent/p3-frost` `988374e`, `assets-src/frost-winter/layout.json`: 37 sprays, lip rime 18, film 0.65). Its report: "the rime is a thin spiky fringe and three small crisp tufts against the approved lumpy drift and large soft feathers"; "the left-shoulder feathers bluish and crisp"; the lip row as tall spikes gave "a picket fence" and was replaced. What to try next: rung 3 of "A still does not match its approved image" (project the approved image onto the glass's frost and correct from the difference), then its rung 4 (new pictures of the feathers with the approved image as the reference); softer, larger feathers whose edges fade into the film. `[winter]`

47. **The trunk and the branches read as smooth orange tubes.** (Round 7, R1, major; `set-A-02-a.jpg`, crops of the rest pair, crop 3 the tree's crown. Also Round 7, R3 part 3, major; `set-G-01.jpg`, winter in the rest state from `macro`, hd, final, and its crops 3, 7, 8.) R1: "The target has finely layered snow-covered boughs, a wide twisted bark trunk and small embedded lights. The candidate has clumpier isolated snow caps, cleaner glossy orange branches and prominent clusters of uniformly bright bulbs." Its fix for this part: "roughen and thicken the organic trunk". R3 part 3: "the wood is smooth tubes with a stretched, swirled painted grain and no bark relief. The knot is a flat painted swirl. The branches look like one smooth cartoon shape, not a carved miniature". R3 part 2, in its major on the crown (`set-F-02.jpg`, crops of the settled still): "the upper branches are smooth barkless striped tubes with light wires cutting across open air". One juror on the settled still: "The tree’s glossy, ribbon-like branches look digitally sculpted rather than like the material of a physical miniature." Tried: ladder move 17 ("The miniature looks like a low detail model", rungs 2 and 3). `tree_closer` (`agent/p3-tree` `ef99abc`, `assets-src/trunk/build.mjs` `STRANDS`): bark drawn as long strands in place of Round 6's plates, hue toward brown, normal scale 1.6. Its report: "Trunk and limbs read as flat copper-orange ribbons with hard dark gaps. Approved is thick, rounded, gnarled bark, dark brown away from the lights, with a root flare"; thicker limbs were "tried and reverted" because the review-tier M3 fell. What to try next: rung 4, the last, of "The miniature looks like a low detail model" (the relief fallback for the trunk): real displaced bark with furrows, ridges and a recessed knot, and thicker limbs at the fork measured again at the final tier, where M3 now has room (0.9203 against 0.75). The crown's snow of the same R1 issue is item 48, its bulbs item 49. `[shared: miniature]`

48. **The crown's snow is clumps and one grey block.** (Round 7, R1, major; `set-A-02-a.jpg`, crops of the rest pair, crop 3. Also Round 7, R3 part 3, major; `set-G-01.jpg`, winter in the rest state from `macro`, hd, final, "crop 2, top right".) R1: "The target has finely layered snow-covered boughs ... The candidate has clumpier isolated snow caps". Its fix: "Shape the snow to more continuous layered boughs". R3 part 3, on "the snow on top of the left tree crown, upper centre": "the snow is a grey rectangular block with near-vertical sides and a flat top. It sits on the foliage like a box or a stray chimney, not like snow that has settled on leaves". Also Round 7, R3 part 2, major (`set-F-02.jpg`, native size crops of winter in the settled state from `hero`, hd, final, crops 2, 4 and 6): "the snow resting on the foliage pads looks like grey sculpted clay blobs with sunken donut holes in the middle and a straight brown stick poking through the lower-right pad". Its fix: "replace the grey donut-shaped snow blobs with bright granular snow mounds draped naturally into the foliage, add bark relief to the upper branches, and wrap the light cord cleanly along the wood". Tried: `tree_closer` (`ef99abc`): 952 smaller clusters (was 718) and 3037 sprigs, hems rounded (`CAP.lip 0.7`), pit grain. Its report: "the snow lies as thick rounded pillows with a stippled grain, not broad smooth blankets; the green is one dense dark mass under each pillow, not layered boughs with lit sprigs; the tiers do not sit where approved's do". Which piece the grey block is was not checked by any builder; the light's closer writes of a "top snow pad" under the star. What to try next: rung 4, the last, of "The miniature looks like a low detail model" (the relief fallback for the crown): the boughs and their blankets laid tier by tier from the approved image; first find the block in the `macro` still and remodel it as the critic asks ("a soft, rounded cap that follows the clumps of the crown"). `[winter]`

49. **The small bulbs are clusters of one brightness.** (Round 7, R1, major; `set-A-02-a.jpg`, crops of the rest pair, crop 3.) From R1's issue on the tree: "small embedded lights" in the target, "prominent clusters of uniformly bright bulbs" in the candidate. Its fix: "make the bulbs smaller and less uniformly bright, with light spilling naturally onto nearby branches". NOT done. The bulbs' bodies and halos are village's and tree's; the grid's cells are 2.9 mm, so it cannot draw a pool per bulb." What to try next: no rung left (rung 3 is the ladder's last). The untried idea, in the light closer's words: "an analytic term for the nearest small lights in `LIGHT_FRAG`; it moves every inside surface and needs a round"; with it a spread of brightness from bulb to bulb, and the string "wound round the fork" that the tree's closer found missing. `[shared: light and post]`

50. **The cottage is bulkier, with coarse cobbles, a vague door and a heavy fence.** (Round 7, R1, major; `set-A-02-a.jpg`, crops of the rest pair, crop 2 the cottage wall and roof.) "The cottage is bulkier, the roof rises higher and has a thick smooth overhang on the right, and the wall is made of chunkier high-contrast cobbles. The target's neatly framed arched door, distinct stepped threshold and delicate fence give way to a less legible door surround and heavier rails and posts." Its fix: "Lower and refine the roof and fascia, use smaller textured masonry, restore the deep arch with vertical wood boards and visible steps, and slim the fence to match the target." Its fix for these: "add weathered wood grain, open pickets, and settled snow caps to the fence, give the windows recessed panes with glass sheen". The snow it asks on the fence and the chimney is a candidate to decline (`r7-ladder.md`): the village's closer writes, under "Image over a critic": "chimney, lamp cap, fence posts and rails are bare". Tried: `village_closer` (`agent/p3-village` `c592dab`, `assets-src/village/build.mjs`, `assets-src/village-winter/build.mjs`): a cobble wall of separate pillowed stones with deep dark joints, the door in brown wood, the fence weathered and bare, ivy on the left wall. Its report: "stones are larger and joints darker than approved"; "The wall above the door is darker than approved"; "The cottage sits slightly larger and the fence lower than approved (layout not moved)". Decision 60 records that the walls were raised from 9.4 to 10.8 mm, and the roof with them by 1.6 mm, so that the apex sits where the approved image has it; R1's "the roof rises higher" stands against that (`r7-ladder.md`). What to try next: rung 3 of "A still does not match its approved image" (project the approved image onto the cottage and correct from the difference): the scale of the stones, the arch and the steps, the fence's rails, and the layout's size of the cottage, which no round has moved. The roof's snow is item 51. `[shared: miniature]`

54. **The collar shows two gold stripes, the ledge snow is flat and the icicles are thin needles.** (Round 7, R1, major; `set-A-02-a.jpg`, crops of the rest pair, crop 5 the collar; "also in set B".) The icicles are mostly thin uniform needles with less interior sparkle than the target's thicker irregular glassy drops." Its fix: "replace the stretched collar snow with fine granular snow and clear wet ice overhanging the lip, and root every icicle in overhanging ice". The first clause of that issue, the glass on the collar, is item 61. One juror: "uniformly placed icicles". Tried: ladder move 14 ("A base looks like plastic or a toy", rung 5, its last). `base_closer` (`agent/p3-base` `a807a2f`; the snow mesh in `2067065`): the collar ring raised 1.3 mm with a groove and a dark contact line, icicles of clear ice, snow heaped in clumps. Its report: icicles "less glassy than approved: no long white streak down the key side of the right ones, the right-most ones thinner and straighter ... tips less bulbous"; the ledge snow "lighter built than approved: the front face thinner and less bright, the clumps smaller"; the ring was not closed at the front because "approved's collar snow is NOT continuous round the front". What to try next: no rung left. The untried work the closer names: "the snow's weight and the brass's top line; one constant per look did not fit the time"; thicker, unequal icicles with bulbous tips and the long streak of the key light. `[winter]`

55. **The base's relief is thin, its right wood pale and its foot in clean bands.** (Round 7, R1, major; `set-A-02-a.jpg`, crops of the rest pair, crop 4 the base from its rim to its foot; "also in set B".) The target has fuller polished leaf relief, more visible wood and softer bronze highlights." Also Round 7, R3 part 2, major (`set-D-03.jpg`, native size crops of winter in the rest state from `base`, hd, final, crop 7): "the pinecone is made of smooth rounded berry-like beads rather than thin woody scales, and it stays dark and completely unfrosted inside a zone where the surrounding wood and carved oak leaves are coated in white rime". Its fix: "remodel the pinecone with crisp overlapping woody bracts integrated into the wood carving and carry the white rime frost across its exposed scales and crevices". What to try next: no rung left ("A base looks like plastic or a toy" stands on rung 5, its last). 56. **In the strokes the storm is an even whiteout with no lag, slam or fold.** (Round 7, R4 part 2, major; `set-E-01.jpg`, one sheet across the whole test shake and the clearing, review tier, frames 78 and 156.) "the snow storm is a uniform milky whiteout that fills the sphere evenly from edge to edge, reading as an opacity fade; there is no lag behind the glass, no slam and no folding storm structure". Its fix: "stage the liquid response: particles stream behind the moving glass, slam against the far wall, fold into a turbulent storm, then turn as one slow whirl". Tried: `liquid_closer` (`agent/p3-liquid` `78e60ec`; `src/globe/liquid.ts` `e9d6869`): the slosh turns the liquid over across the first strokes. Its report: "The lag of the liquid shows in the lift and on the first stroke only. Once the whirl is full (1.15 s) the snow lags the glass by about 6 to 8 mm at the later reversals"; "The whirl's axis stands 49 degrees off the view axis (held by the \"flat\" pin); the approved shake picture shows rings about the view axis." Frame 78 lies inside the swap window (frames 72 to 107 in `gate.log`, G11), where measure M4 asks the miniature hidden (0.9922 against 0.85); see `r7-ladder.md`. What to try next: no rung left on "The shake looks keyed, light or mechanical" (its rung 2 is the lag; the ladder stands on rung 5). The untried ideas: the lag carried to the later reversals; a ruling on the "flat" pin so the whirl can turn about the view axis; and a sheet that shows the strokes' storm on more than one frame. `[shared: liquid]`

Also the last sentence of R4 part 1's major on `set-D-02.jpg`.) R4 part 2: "the fog clears as a global fade across the whole sphere at once rather than snow falling and settling downward; visibility returns everywhere simultaneously". The liquid's report: "In the clearing the near field empties: the front of the dome holds 0.75 of an even field at the landing, 0.42 at +1 s, 0.14 at +3 s." What to try next: rung 5, the last, of "The particles look like confetti or a filter" (raise the count toward `particles.tuneMax`), spent on near, large flakes from +1 to +5 s; and the untried state both closers name: flakes that are seen to land and lie on the roof, the branches and the ground, with the haze thinning from the top down. `[shared: liquid]`

"despite very shallow macro depth of field on the table and background bokeh, none of the snow particles blur out of focus near the glass; sharp small flecks sit directly over the blurred foreground snow bank". What to try next: rung 1 of "The particles look like confetti or a filter" names it (spread them through the whole depth of the liquid and let the lens blur the near ones). The untried middle between the two rounds: keep the flakes over the miniature small and sharp, as M3 asks, and let the few flakes at the front glass outside the miniature's outline take the lens's blur in varied, irregular shapes. `[shared: particles' drawing]`

63. **Sound: thirteen effects and both candidate soundtracks are not made, because the sound tool's account has no credits.** (Phase 4, 02:52 UTC Nov 25; not a critic's issue; decision 78.) The sound agent's words: "the ElevenLabs account is out of credits, so no take can be made. Round 8: "the shake has no globe motion: the sphere and base hold the identical resting pose in every frame — no lift with effort, no unequal strokes, no reversals, no lagging tilt, no set-down or settle; the storm simply erupts in the glass between 10.03 s (fully calm) and 10.23 s (left half already fogged) while the globe sits dead still, so it reads as an interior effect over a weightless prop, not a heavy thing shaken in a hand". Round 9: "the strokes reverse and are unequal, and the interior does storm, but the globe stays nearly upright at a flat height through the reversals, then meets the table while still traveling sideways and levels instead of landing and settling. It does not move like a heavy thing in a hand." Their fixes: "animate the globe itself through the shake: a heavy lift, uneven stroke lengths with sharp reversals and a tilt that lags the hand, then a set-down that lands with a small compress-and-rock settle before the storm peaks"; "lag a tilt behind each reversal, arc the lift, and let the set-down land and rock to rest without a sideways skate". Logged 03:54 UTC, not fixed in these rounds: the shake itself is a shared system proven on winter (`src/shake/table.ts`), and a shared system is not reworked inside a season's round. Contested in part: `shake.liftMm` (7.25) and `shake.tiltDeg` (6) were lowered for winter's rounds (decision 45: the lifted glass left the frame and the tilted foot went into the surface, with the camera locked through a shake by Brandon's own choice), and the sideways carry into the set down was made for Round 4 (decision 59); those are not changed again. On the sheets the globe does travel left and right by the stroke's length; what is missing is height and tilt. What to try next, in the polish rounds, inside the same lift and tilt: let the tilt lag each reversal by a few frames and reach its cap on every stroke, give the lift and the set down an arc, end the sideways travel before the foot touches, and let the landing's overshoot in tilt show for the whole of `shake.landSettleSeconds`. `[shared: shake]`

## For Brandon to decide

Things only he can settle. Nothing waits on them.

1. Music and the sound tool's plan. The soundtrack is made with ElevenLabs on the Creator plan. Its terms allow commercial use of music "except film, TV, radio, and Studio Games" and do not define "film". The same plan is marked for individual use only: companies appear from the Scale plan up. So the terms do not clearly allow this music in a video a company posts on social media. Both soundtrack candidates are made and offered at the review anyway. Your choices: approve as it is, move the account to a plan whose music terms cover it, or supply a track of your own, which replaces the generated music with no other change. Quotes: `references/terms/sound/QUOTES.md`.
2. The repo is public and the config expected it to be private. Because the sound tool's terms forbid making its output available as separate audio files, no generated sound file is committed. They live in the workspace and in the published app, and an archive is attached to the thread. If you make the repo private, say so and the kept sounds will be committed.
3. The approved images and Seedream 5 Pro. The terms that could be read do not clearly allow its output in a published video and on a public website: it depends on which route the image tool uses to reach the model, which is not published, and on the plan of this account. Nothing was changed because of this, as the kit says. New pictures that ship are made with models whose terms allow both. Quotes: `references/terms/images/TERMS.md`.
4. The plan of this account. The image tool's own terms allow commercial use of generated pictures on Enterprise Pro and Enterprise Max, and only personal use on individual plans. The account belongs to an organization, but the plan was not measured.
5. The hosting plan. Vercel's Hobby plan is for personal or non-commercial use only. A company portfolio piece may need the Pro plan. The limit behind `host.maxShippedMb` counts the source files uploaded by the command line tool, not the built site and not a deploy from Git, so the key is safe as it is and was not changed. Details: `references/terms/hosting/LIMITS.md`.

6. The hero framing and the lift of the shake (decision 29). The approved images show the globe with about 9 percent of the frame under its foot and 6 percent above the glass. The shake lifts the globe by a tenth of the frame's height in front of a locked camera, and the kit's checks do not let the camera stand further back than it now does. So the globe sits lower in the frame than in the approved images, with less of the surface in front of the base, and stays whole while it is shaken. The other choice is the approved framing with the top of the glass leaving the picture for about a second in each shake. It is one number in the config (`cameras.hero.aimHeightMm`, 74 now, about 66 for the approved framing).

- Item 6, added 2026-11-24 10:42 UTC: decision 45 lowers the lift and the tilt of the shake below the range the kit gives the Builder (expected about 6.5 mm and 6 degrees against the kit's 18 and 14), so that the lifted glass keeps about 5 percent of the frame above it, as the blockout critic asked. If you want the larger shake back, the choices are: accept the glass within half a percent of the top edge, let the camera stand further back than the fill check allows, or let the camera move with the lift.

7. The coding of the app's textures (decision 56, 2026-11-24 16:16 UTC). The kit asks for a compressed GPU format; measured here it made the files larger and the normal and height maps less exact, and the memory it would save is not needed (129 of 500 MB). The app ships near-lossless WebP instead. Say if the GPU format is wanted regardless, for phones with little memory; it is one more coding in the pack tool and one branch in the loader.
